# CLAUDE.md

This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.

## What is Prism?

Prism is a universal interface for documents, messages, tasks, calendar, and knowledge management, backed by a Parachute vault (SQLite knowledge graph at localhost:1940). It ships in three shells over one shared React 19 UI core:

- **Desktop** (`apps/desktop`) — **LEGACY since WP4.3: kept building as the rollback path only; Prism Client replaces it on every Mac (`apps/desktop/README.md`, parity table `docs/roadmap/architecture-v2/desktop-parity.md`).** Tauri 2.x (Rust backend + React frontend). The host-mode app that holds a vault token; talks to Parachute (and Matrix/Google/Claude/Notion) directly.
- **Web** (`apps/web`) — a static PWA (mobile + desktop browser) for editing the vault from anywhere and **Google-Docs-style sharing**. The browser holds **no vault credentials**; it talks only to the **Prism Server** gateway.
- **Client** (`apps/client`, Arch v2 WP4.1) — "Prism Client", a thin Tauri 2 shell bundling the **native** web build (`apps/web/dist-native`). It signs in via system-browser PKCE with a loopback redirect, keeps the `pd_…` device token in the Keychain, and talks to ONE configured Prism Server. There is no vault token, no ingest and no CLI spawning, and it exposes just 9 IPC commands to the main window (the ninth, `save_export`, since w11-native) (+1 to the separate quick-capture window, WP4.2: tray + global-shortcut quick capture, notifications, export, drag-drop; see docs/client-app.md "Native extras"). It installs side by side with the legacy desktop (`com.benjaminlife.prism.client`). iOS joins this shell in WP5 (merged as code, see *iOS app* below — never compiled for iOS or run on a device yet). **Gotcha:** it is Tauri, but `isDesktop` (`packages/core/src/lib/platform.ts`) is false there because `window.__PRISM_HOST__` is present — keep that check if you touch shell detection. Its CSP allows only the server for connect/img (page script can read the bearer); external note images and the OpenFreeMap basemap therefore come through the server's SSRF-guarded proxies (Client parity C, below) — never widen the client CSP for them. Docs: `docs/client-app.md`.

### Monorepo layout (npm workspaces)

```
packages/core         Shared React UI: renderers, editor, layout, stores, VaultClient seam
apps/desktop          LEGACY Tauri host-mode shell (rollback only since WP4.3; src-tauri lives here)
apps/web              Vite PWA shell (HttpVaultClient → Prism Server gateway); `build:native` → dist-native for apps/client
apps/client           Thin Tauri client shell (native web build + device token + Keychain; macOS now, iOS in WP5)
apps/server           Prism Server — Node home-server: auth + permission gateway + (P3) collab
apps/collab-server    Cloudflare Worker (Yjs) — RETIRED, superseded by apps/server collab
```

**VaultClient seam** (`packages/core/src/data/`): the UI core depends on a `VaultClient` interface, dependency-injected per shell — `TauriVaultClient` (invoke) for desktop, `HttpVaultClient` (fetch → gateway) for web. One UI, two transports.

## Live topology (as of 2026-10-01 cutover)

The Mac mini's **Prism Server** (pm2 `prism-server`) owns ALL ingest: Matrix, ClickUp, Fireflies, Fathom, embedding index, **calendar** (`CALENDAR_SYNC_ENABLED=true`, `CALENDAR_DELETE_MODE=log` → `archive` after a clean day), **Proton mail** (`PROTON_SYNC_ENABLED=true`; the `com.omniharmonic.proton-mail` launchd job running `proton_mail.py` is booted out + disabled — its `setup`/`health`/`search` CLI still work; omniharmonic_agent `check_oauth.py` reads ingest freshness from `/acl/workers`), and the **skill scheduler** (`SKILLS_ENABLED=true`). The desktop `Prism.app` on the mini runs with `ingest_mode: "client"` (starts no services/scheduler). Verify any time: `PRISM_OWNER_TOKEN=… scripts/check-desktop-independence.sh` (exit 0 = every active source server-owned + healthy). Rollback per source = the cutover runbooks below (flag off → restart pm2 → re-enable the desktop flag / `launchctl enable` + `bootstrap` the proton plist). Gmail ingest stays off (email is Proton; `assertConfig` refuses Gmail+Proton both on). Live actions (`ACTIONS_*_ENABLED`) are built but OFF.

**Clients (WP4.3).** The Mac mini and the laptop switch to **Prism Client** by the runbook in `docs/client-app.md` ("Switch-over runbook": back up + archive `Prism.app`, blank `parachute_api_key`/`collab_token`, revoke the old token if unshared). Acceptance per Mac: `scripts/check-client-no-vault-token.sh` (read-only; config dirs + Prism process envs, prints locations never values) exits 0 and `/acl/workers` is green. Rollback = restore the archived app + config backup. Known client gaps vs the desktop (GitHub folder sync, Notion DB sync, live actions off) are in `desktop-parity.md`; parity A (branch `parity/a`) ported calendar edit/delete, the skill config card, cancelling server skill runs, interactive model routing (server LM Studio) and the vault-wide wikilink resolve.

**External images + basemap in the client (Client parity C, 🔒 SSRF surface).** `apps/server/src/routes/media.ts` + `src/media/{netguard,fetcher,sniff,cache}.ts`: `GET /api/media/proxy?u=` (one raster image) and `GET /api/map/style/:id` + `/api/map/ofm/*` (OpenFreeMap only: host pinned to `tiles.openfreemap.org`, path regex allowlist, TileJSON inlined, asset URLs rewritten to `/api/map/ofm/…`, a style with any foreign URL → 502). **Signed-in only** (`requestVia` session/device/local-token; capability links, anon, MCP → 401). SSRF rules: https:443 only (`MEDIA_PROXY_HTTP_HOSTS`/`MEDIA_PROXY_PORTS` opt-ins), DNS names only (EVERY IP literal refused — WHATWG already normalised decimal/hex/octal forms), no `localhost`/`.local`/`.internal`/single-label, never our own/vault/hub host; the server resolves DNS itself — c-ares `dns.promises.Resolver` (timeout/tries, `cancel()` at the deadline), NEVER `dns.lookup` (getaddrinfo blocks libuv's 4-thread pool → fs + scrypt/login stall) — negative-caches failures 60 s, and refuses the host if ANY answer is non-public (`isPublicAddress`: RFC1918, loopback, link-local/metadata, CGNAT, multicast, reserved, docs, benchmarking, 0/8, IPv6 outside 2000::/3 incl. mapped/NAT64/ULA/link-local, Teredo/6to4/docs); then dials THAT IP with SNI/cert/Host = the name and `agent:false` (no env-proxy re-resolution; test pins it) (no rebinding window); redirects manual, ≤3, each hop fully re-validated; no client headers/cookies forwarded; one deadline over connect+body (slow drip → 504); 5 MB cap (declared, streamed, decompressed); type must be `image/*`/octet-stream AND sniff as PNG/JPEG/GIF/WebP/AVIF/BMP/ICO — **SVG refused**; responses rebuilt (sniffed type, nosniff, `CSP: default-src 'none'; sandbox` — `app.ts` no longer overwrites a route's own CSP —, inline disposition, CORP same-origin, private max-age). Per-user limits `MEDIA_PROXY_PER_MINUTE` (240) / `MAP_PROXY_PER_MINUTE` (1500); SEPARATE media/map pools each with a global AND per-user in-flight cap (`MEDIA_PROXY_MAX_INFLIGHT` 12 / `MEDIA_PROXY_PER_USER_INFLIGHT` 3, `MAP_PROXY_MAX_INFLIGHT` 16 / `MAP_PROXY_PER_USER_INFLIGHT` 4, bounded wait `MEDIA_PROXY_QUEUE_WAIT_MS` → 503), in-flight coalescing, per-URL failure memory 60 s; every post-parse refusal answers ONE generic `refused` (no internal-DNS oracle); the map style's rewritten templates must each match `classifyMapPath` (fail closed) and the client `protocolUrlToPath` refuses `%2e/%2f/%5c` + checks the normalised path stays under `/api/map/{style,ofm}/`; image fetches reveal the server IP + view timing to image hosts (documented L2); on-disk LRU `MEDIA_CACHE_DIR` (default `media-cache/` beside the DB, 0700/0600, SHA-256 keys) capped by `MEDIA_CACHE_MAX_BYTES` (512 MB). Off switches `MEDIA_PROXY_ENABLED`/`MAP_PROXY_ENABLED=false`. **Client (native only):** `apps/web/src/native/externalImages.ts` — a document-wide MutationObserver re-points external `<img src>` to `blob:` URLs fetched via `serverFetch` (bearer in a header; NO signed URL token by design; original kept in `data-prism-src`; ProseMirror ignores leaf attribute mutations so stored HTML keeps the original URL); the basemap uses `@prism/core` `setMapProxyFetch` (installed in `transport.ts` native branch) → `CommonsMap` swaps OpenFreeMap style URLs for `prismmap://style/<id>` and registers a MapLibre `addProtocol` handler (`components/map/mapProxy.ts`). The PWA loads both directly (unchanged). Tests: `test/media-proxy.test.ts` (resolver + transport injected, no internet), `npm run verify:media -w @prism/web`; `verify-client.mjs` + `origin.rs` pin `img-src` to `'self' data: blob: <server>`. Residual: custom style URLs, `<picture><source>`/CSS background images, website-note iframes stay blocked in the client.

## Commands

```bash
# Desktop (Tauri) — run from apps/desktop
cd apps/desktop && npm run tauri dev      # Dev mode with hot reload
cd apps/desktop && npm run tauri build    # Production bundle
cd apps/desktop/src-tauri && cargo check  # Rust check

# Prism Client (thin Tauri shell) — run from apps/client; set CARGO_TARGET_DIR in sandboxes
cd apps/client && npm run tauri build -- --bundles app   # builds apps/web native first → "Prism Client.app"
cd apps/client/src-tauri && cargo test                   # PKCE / loopback / origin+CSP / token exchange (local fakes)
node apps/client/scripts/verify-client.mjs               # CSP, capabilities, identity, bundle invariants (--build)

# Web PWA
npm run build -w @prism/web               # Build static PWA → apps/web/dist
npm run dev -w @prism/web                  # Vite dev server

# Prism Server (the gateway) — run from apps/server
cd apps/server && npm run dev              # node --env-file=.env --watch (needs .env; see below)
cd apps/server && npm run typecheck        # tsc --noEmit
cd apps/server && node --env-file=.env --import tsx scripts/verify-gateway.ts   # security e2e

# Type check everything
npx tsc --noEmit                           # (per-workspace tsconfigs exist too)
```

There are no unit test suites; verification is typecheck + build + run-against-the-live-vault (curl / Playwright). `apps/server/scripts/verify-gateway.ts` is the gateway security check.

## Architecture

```
Frontend (React/TypeScript)     ← Tauri IPC →     Backend (Rust)
├── components/renderers/       ← invoke() →      ├── commands/        (IPC handlers)
├── components/layout/                             ├── clients/         (HTTP: parachute, matrix, google, claude, ollama)
├── app/stores/ (Zustand)                          ├── services/        (background tokio tasks)
├── lib/parachute/ (API hooks)                     ├── sync/adapters/   (Google Docs, Notion, GitHub)
└── lib/dashboard/ (filter engine)                 └── models/          (Rust data types)
```

### Key Principles

1. **Parachute owns the data.** Never touch SQLite directly. All reads/writes go via HTTP API at localhost:1940.
2. **Tags are the type system.** Notes are typed by tags (`task`, `meeting`, `person`, `project`, etc.), not `metadata.type`. The `inferContentType()` function in `src/lib/schemas/content-types.ts` maps tags → `ContentType` → renderer.
3. **Frontend talks only to Rust.** All external API calls (Matrix, Google, Claude, Notion) happen in Rust commands. Frontend calls `invoke()` only.
4. **Agent uses Claude Code CLI — hardened.** The `ClaudeClient` and `DispatchManager` spawn `claude -p` subprocesses (not the Anthropic HTTP API), and EVERY desktop spawn is built by `src-tauri/src/clients/claude_args.rs` (WP0.1d, mirrors the server runner): no built-in tools, ONLY the active vault's MCP via a per-run 0600 config, an explicit per-feature vault-tool allowlist under `dontAsk`, no settings sources, a fixed EMPTY cwd under the app-data dir (NOT the repo root), env allowlist. Never hand-build a claude argv or re-add `--dangerously-skip-permissions`. See *Desktop agent runner* below.
5. **Tauri commands return `Result<T, PrismError>`.** `PrismError` serializes to a string for the frontend. All new commands must follow this pattern.

## Prism Server & Web Sharing (the security gateway)

`apps/server` is a Node home-server (Hono + better-sqlite3) that is the **single trust boundary** for the web/shared path. It is the **only** component that holds the vault token; clients never get one.

```
Browser ── session cookie OR capability link ──▶ PRISM SERVER (apps/server) ── vault token ──▶ Parachute
          (httpOnly, no token in JS)              ├ /auth/*  magic-link sign-in, sessions, logout, me
                                                   ├ /api/*   permission gateway (authorize every read/write)
                                                   └ /*       serves the built web PWA (same-origin)
```

**Auth (`src/auth/`).** Three actor kinds resolved per request (`actor.ts`): a signed-in **user** (httpOnly session cookie → SQLite row, `session.ts`; or a native **device token** `Bearer pd_…`, see below), an **anyone-with-link capability** (HMAC-signed token naming a grant id, `capability.ts`), or **anon**. Auth is **invite-only with passwords** — there is no open self-signup:
- **Owner** (`OWNER_EMAIL`) signs in via **magic link**, which is **owner-only** (gated at both `/auth/request` and `/auth/callback`) — bootstrap/recovery, since the owner controls that inbox.
- **Everyone else**: the owner issues an **invite** (`invite.ts` — single-use, hashed, 7-day) → recipient **registers** a password account (`password.ts` — scrypt) at `/accept-invite?token=` → logs in with email + password (`/auth/login`). Sharing by email (`/acl` people endpoints) auto-invites, so a grant binds to a real authenticated account, not a bare email. Generic 401s avoid account enumeration; `/auth/login` + `/auth/register` are rate-limited.
- Email via **Resend** (`RESEND_API_KEY`, shared sender in `email.ts`); with no key, links log to the console (dev only). A signed-in **non-owner with no grants sees nothing** (empty `/api/notes`, 403 on notes + the graph) — authentication never implies authorization.
- **Native clients: device tokens (WP2.1, `auth/device.ts` + `routes/device.ts`, full spec `docs/native-auth.md`).** RFC 8252 + PKCE S256 (plain refused): the app opens `/auth/device/authorize` in the system browser. `redirect_uri` must exactly match `DEVICE_REDIRECT_URIS` (default `prism://auth/callback`) or be a loopback `http://127.0.0.1|[::1]:<port ≥1024>/callback` (or `/`) with no query. EVERY pre-consent error gets a 400 page, never a redirect. The params are parked server-side (`device_auth_requests` + an httpOnly `prism_device_req` cookie). A signed-out browser goes to `/?next=/auth/device/continue`. `postLoginTarget()` honors ONLY that exact path, and `/auth/callback` resumes it after a magic link. The consent POST is bound to the session, a CSRF HMAC, and the parking cookie. Then `/auth/device/token` exchanges code + verifier (5-min single-use code; a replay revokes the minted token; rate-limited) for `pd_…`. Only its SHA-256 is stored (`device_tokens`); it has a 90-day sliding expiry with a 365-day cap. `resolveActor` checks `Bearer pd_…` right after the session cookie and yields the SAME user actor (role + grants). It is honored over the tunnel, unlike the loopback-only COLLAB_TOKEN branch, which is unchanged and never sees a `pd_` token. Collab `resolveLevel` accepts it as the Hocuspocus `token`. Tauri origins (`NATIVE_ORIGINS`) get **non-credentialed** CORS; the APP_ORIGIN cookie rule is untouched. Manage devices at `GET /auth/devices` and `DELETE /auth/devices/:id` (own only; the server owner may use `?all=1` and revoke any), shown as **Settings → Account → Signed-in devices** through the optional `AccountClient.listDevices/revokeDevice` seam. The consent page shows the label as a claim ("An app calling itself …") and the redirect target prominently. **Hardening:** `/auth/change-password` via a device token requires the current password and can NEVER set a first password (browser session only). A successful change revokes the account's OTHER devices. MCP tokens minted through a device (`mcp_tokens.device_id`) are revoked with it (`revokeDevice`, through the `mcp-token` revoker). Capability links made through a device persist, like a session's. `consentCsrf` throws without `SESSION_SECRET`.

**Permissions (`src/permissions.ts`).** Levels `view < comment < suggest < edit < own`. `effectiveLevel(grants, note, isOwner)` = max over grants matching the note **id** or any of its **tags**, with owner → `own`. Grants live in SQLite (`db.ts`): `(subject_type: user|link|anyone, subject, resource_type: note|tag|page|…, resource, level)`.

**Page-subtree grants (wave 2D, NP-CO-09).** `resource_type: "page"`, `resource` = a page's NOTE ID: matches that page and every note whose path is under the page's CURRENT path ("sharing a page shares its sub-pages"). The anchor's path is resolved synchronously from the tree projection (`tree.ts` registers `setPageAnchorResolver`; `warmPageAnchors(grants)` loads it before a decision — gateway middleware + collab `resolveLevel`); unknown/trashed anchor → matches the anchor id ONLY (fail closed). The grant follows the id: moving the shared page keeps its subtree shared, moving a sub-page out drops inherited access, a new page at the old path inherits nothing. Among page grants the NEAREST shared ancestor wins (that is how a child is restricted or expanded); note/tag/space/vault grants and the role floor still union. Never for private notes (except a page grant ON the private page = an explicit share). `NoteRef.path` is threaded through every `effectiveCaps` call site — a ref without `path` matches page grants by id only, so a NEW call site must pass it. ACL: `PUT /acl/notes/:id/people {scope:"page"|"note"}` (default = the person's existing kind; one grant per person per page), `DELETE` removes both kinds; a scoped (non-admin) sharer may make a page share only while holding the granted caps + `share` on every current sub-page (refusal names none); `GET /acl/notes/:id` adds `owner`, `people[].{name,avatar,scope}`, `inherited[]` (per person, nearest ancestor, with `from`), `parent`; canonical ids only. Names/avatars go only to callers who already see the access list (admins, `share` holders); the owner row's email to admins only (`src/sharing.ts` NAMING POLICY). **Moves and page shares (review C1/H1):** a non-owner PATCH may NOT change `path` (`organize` → 403 `move_required`; an editor's stray path is ignored) — every move goes through `POST /api/notes/:id/move` (the MCP `prism_update_note` path change too), which refuses, for non-admins, (a) landing a page that carries page grants over EXISTING notes and (b) landing the group under a page shared with others that does not already cover it, unless the mover holds `share` on every newly exposed note (generic refusal; admin bypass audited as `pages.move-share-bypass`). **Scoped sharers (M2)** cannot change/remove a grant made by an admin/owner/governance, replace another sharer's grant of the other kind, or lower anyone's current access on the page or its sub-pages (`scopedSharerRefusal`). `access-preview` 404s unless `parent` is a viewable page the caller can add to; only viewable ancestors count (M1). `GET /api/notes` for page grantees: ≤ `PAGE_GRANT_LISTINGS` (25) subtree listings, `PAGE_GRANT_LIST_PER_MINUTE` (30) per caller. **Re-review round 2.** *Nearest-wins consequences (L-6):* a page grant on a sub-page REPLACES what its holder inherits from above (it can restrict as well as expand) — so anything that puts a nearer anchor above notes is access-changing: the move route also refuses a non-admin move that would LOWER another person's page-inherited access on a note that still inherits (`lowers`; admin bypass audited), and a scoped sharer's new/changed grant may never leave the person with less than they have WITHOUT that sharer's own grants on the resource (they may freely lower/remove what THEY gave — also on `PUT/DELETE /acl/tags/:tag/people`, where a grant made by an admin/owner/governance or by anyone else is untouchable). *Trash (H-A/M-D):* a TRASHED anchor still resolves but reaches only notes that are in the Trash themselves (so whoever trashed a shared page can see and restore the group), live notes under its path inherit nothing; restore runs `restoreExposure` (live notes now under the restored paths need the restorer's `share`; admin bypass audited `pages.restore-share-bypass`); non-admin moves under a trashed page's path are refused. *Own-share growth (H-B):* moving a note under ANY newly covering page share needs `share` on it when others gain access OR the mover's own caps on it would grow. *Creates:* `createCapsAt(actor, path, tags)` (`sharing.ts`) = the caps for a NEW note at a path (page shares on ancestors count; empty under a trashed page) — the create handler's rule is `createCapsAt(...).has("create")`. *Identity (M-A/M-B/L-4):* a non-admin never receives an email from metadata — `writerNames(allowEmail)` yields emails only for admins; `/api/query` gives non-admins display names (or nothing) for writer AND creator and drops `prism_last_change`; `prism_creator` is removed from non-admin note responses in favour of `_creator {me, name}`; `/acl/notes/:id` `note.creator` is null for non-admins (`createdByMe` instead), `inherited[]`/`parent`/activity/access-preview count only ancestors the caller can VIEW and carry emails for admins only. *Listing (L-3):* `/api/notes` expands 25 outermost shared pages per request; more → `X-Prism-Truncated: shared-pages` + `X-Prism-Shared-Pages-Next: <offset>`, continue with `?shared_pages_offset=`. Tests: `test/page-grants.test.ts`, `test/sharing-security.test.ts`.

**Sharing reads (`routes/sharing.ts`, before the owner short-circuit, per-actor 60/min).** `GET /api/shared-with-me` (signed-in users; top-level note/page grants still viewable + tag grants; `sharedBy` = display name only), `GET /api/comments?note=&unresolved=1&mine=1&limit=` (threads from the live doc or SQLite snapshot of VIEWABLE pages only — no actor ids, `mine` computed server-side; ≤400 docs scanned), `GET /api/notes/:id/activity` (comments + shares (shares only for access managers) + `lastEditor`), `GET /api/notes/:id/access-preview?parent=` (who would lose/gain inherited access by a move; names only for access managers). **Writer attribution (server-owned, review M3):** the stamp is 2C's `writer-stamp.ts` (`prism_last_writer` = OPAQUE `u_<hmac>` id or `"link"`, `prism_last_write_at`) plus `prism_last_change` = `<kind>@<write_at>` (`sharing.ts` `writerStamp`/`changeKindOf`; kind `edit` | `suggestion` human command | `agent` Prism MCP | `accepted-suggestion` heuristic; a kind bound to an older stamp time is ignored). Stamped by collab stores (most recent writer; server-internal stores leave it alone) and gateway content writes (`agent` when `requestVia` is `mcp`). Client values for the three keys are DROPPED on non-owner PATCH/create (`/api/properties` refuses `prism_*`), and the keys are stripped from every non-owner note response (`forViewer`/`annotate` in api.ts; version rows also drop `prism_trashed_by`; links additionally `stripIdentity`). Non-owner version rows carry `writer: {kind,name,self}`; names resolve through `writerNames()` for SIGNED-IN viewers only (an account whose name is its email reads as unnamed to others). `GET /api/notes/:id/activity` adds `writers` (names of THIS page's stamps) + `me` so the owner passthrough's raw stamps resolve client-side (`@prism/core` `writerOf(input, directory)`, `usePageActivity`). Tests: `test/sharing-routes.test.ts`. Client: `components/sharing/*` (SharedWithMe, PresenceAvatars, PageInfo, PageUpdates, MoveAccessNotice, PersonAvatar), VaultClient optional `listSharedWithMe/listComments/getPageActivity/getAccessPreview`.

**2D ⇄ create-allowlist integration (merge of main 632abe9).** A non-owner path change stays `move_required` (PATCH never moves; main's `placementRefusal` block for PATCH is gone — main's tests now drive the same destinations through `POST /notes/:id/move`, where a trashed parent is 403 and a plain folder is the owner's). `destinationParentRefusal` (pages.ts, THE create/move placement hook) also counts standing AT THE PLACE: `createCapsAt(actor, target, [])` (page share on any ancestor, vault grant, role floor; empty under a trashed page) — tags are deliberately not passed, a tag grant is not standing inside a page. A CREATE into a plain folder that lies under a page somebody shares (`sharedAncestor`, tree projection) needs `create` there: 404 if the creator cannot view that page, else 403; other plain folders / the top level stay free for tag members, and the M4 database-row rule is unchanged. `POST /api/notes` accepts `create` from the tag slice OR from `createCapsAt(actor, normalizedPath, tags)` (a page-share holder with `create` adds sub-pages). `forViewer` lives in `sharing.ts` and is applied to `routes/search.ts` rows too (a non-admin keeps `prism_creator` only when it is their OWN address); the search `author=` filter accepts only the caller's own address from a non-admin (anything else → `[]`, no creator-email oracle). `routes/search.ts`, `routes/attachments.ts`, `routes/governance.ts` view checks pass `NoteRef.path` (+ `warmPageAnchors` where they sit outside the `api` middleware). **Client mounts:** `Navigation` renders `SharedWithMe` and, for a guest (`useViewerIsGuest` → `CollabSharing.getViewer().role === "guest"`; false while unknown/on error), hides Messages / agent / tools / Pages / Tools / New page / Trash; `CollabDoc` passes `PresenceAvatars` through `PageHeader`'s `presence` slot; the top-bar ⋯ (`PageActionsTrigger`) ends with `PageInfo`; `MovePageDialog` asks `useMoveAccessCheck` before a move and shows `MoveAccessNotice` + "Move to … anyway" when access changes. **Inside the workspace a suggest-level person does NOT get the live doc**: `Canvas` `reviewMode` = "propose" routes them to the local propose-for-review draft (P4); the live suggest-only editor (commands) is the `/collab/:id` page. Specs: `pages-nav.spec.ts` (mounts), `suggest-only.spec.ts` (merged-editor walk, empty page, phone, in-workspace draft; fixture `collab-route.html?app` boots the workspace against the real-server fixture).

**Final 2D review fixes.** *Ingest keys are one module* (`apps/server/src/ingest-keys.ts`: `INGEST_KEYS` + `sourceId`, `ingestKeyChanged(key, value, current)`): a non-admin can change none of them through gateway create/PATCH (dropped) or `POST /api/properties/:id` + `/properties/batch` (403 `forbidden` per item); restating the stored value is fine. `source` stays an ordinary property — only the values an ingester keys on (`INGEST_SOURCES`: clickup, fireflies, fathom, proton-bridge, github, gmail, matrix, calendar, notion) are reserved. The CSV import is admin-only and `/notes/:id/meta` accepts only lock/order/style, so neither needs the rule. *Page shares and the Trash:* a live page grant DOES reach trashed notes under the page (a holder must see and restore what was trashed there); the anti-escalation check for a scoped sharer (`pageShareRefusal`) therefore runs over `descendantRefs` — a FRESH `path_prefix` listing, trashed notes included — not the cached tree. *Database rows under a shared page* (`destinationParentRefusal`): the M4 rule (source tags + `create` in each, view on the database page) does not apply when the database is, or sits under, a page somebody shares — `create` standing at the place is required. *Access-request approval* raises an existing grant in place: `upsertGrant` never rewrites `created_by` (pinned in `test/notifications.test.ts`). *Emails:* `forViewer` also strips `prism_trashed_by` / `forked_by` unless they are the viewer's own (`forked_by` joined `IDENTITY_KEYS`, so links/public/GitHub lose it too); presence, command-endpoint and MCP-agent authorship use the display name or the neutral "Member" (client `identityFrom` and server agree, so "my comment" checks still match) — never the account email. **Accepted, not changed (final review):** (L5) a member can learn that a hidden note EXISTS at a path — a create at a taken path answers 409, and a trash/move of a group with hidden descendants answers 403 "can't change every page in this group"; accepted. (L6) a holder of `create`/`organize` in a published or shared tag can add that tag to a note they can edit without holding `share` on it — by design (that is what organize in a tag means). Also left as is, known adjacent gap: an MCP agent's suggestion/comment still stores `data-actor-id` = the account email in the shared document (the human command path stores an opaque `h_…` id).

**Gateway (`src/routes/api.ts`).** The owner short-circuits to a **transparent passthrough** (`proxyToVault`) — full vault, token-free, so the desktop-grade app works unchanged in the browser. Non-owners hit only allowlisted routes (notes/search/tags), each filtered by `effectiveLevel`; **every other `/api` path → 403**. `effectiveLevel` is the authoritative guard — tag queries only *narrow* what's fetched. (The server holds a whole-vault token, so the vault's own tag-scoping never applies to gateway traffic — Prism's permission math is the only guard. The old "REST tag-scope gap, vault #404" note was wrong: #404 was a test gap; the vault does enforce `scoped_tags` on REST.) **Version history routes** (`GET /notes/:id/versions[/:ix]` = `view`, `POST /notes/:id/restore` = `edit`, see *Version history* below) are allowlisted too; a non-owner restore may not change `prism_creator`/`prism_visibility`, and vault `actor`/`via` is stripped for non-owners. **Owner reads are coalesced** (`coalescedGet` in `api.ts`): identical in-flight GETs share one vault call and a 200 is reused for `GATEWAY_READ_TTL_MS` (5 s); any owner write through the gateway clears the cache. Reason: vault ≥0.7.9 runs schema validation on every note of every list (no opt-out), so a full-vault list (the tree, ~16 MB) costs seconds of the single-threaded vault — N tabs/devices/retries queued N copies and a swapping host stalled for minutes (2026-09-30 incident). Diagnose with `PRISM_VAULT_TRACE=1` (logs every proxied + server-side vault call with caller and duration). `vaultErr` passes the vault's 400/413/422 through (`vault_rejected` + `reason`); 401/403 still map to 502 (they describe the server's token). **Non-owner writes are built from allowlists — never forward a raw body or query to the vault** (2026-10 hotfix: the member `POST /notes` spread its body, and the vault honours `notes: [...]` batches, per-item `if_exists: replace|update`, `id`, `links`, `created_at` → one `create` grant could overwrite/retag any note). Non-owner create = strict schema (`content`, `path`, `tags`, `metadata`; anything else 400 `invalid_request`; batch is owner-only), always `if_exists: "error"`, a taken path → one generic 409 (`pathUnavailable`), path through `placementRefusal` (`pages.ts`: `normalizePagePath` + `isProtectedPath` + `exportedLocation` + not under a trashed page — also applied to a non-owner path PATCH and a governed `new_entry`), no system tags (`protectionReason`) and no published tag without create standing in it; `vaultClient.createNote` builds its body key by key. Tests: `test/create-allowlist.test.ts` (the fake vault honours `notes`/`if_exists`/`id` like 0.7.9). **Round 2 (same hotfix).** (a) *Create placement:* a non-owner create with a `path` passes `placementRefusal` and, when the destination's parent is an existing PAGE note, needs `create`/`organize` on that page (unviewable page → 404, viewable without the cap → 403); a plain folder or the top level is free as before (New menu, `vault/imports/<day>/…`, "Open as database"). `destinationParentRefusal` (`pages.ts`) is the single hook — the MOVE route calls it with `requirePage: true` (top level / plain folder = owner only, unchanged); the sharing branch swaps its caps lookup for `createCapsAt(actor, path, tags)` (`sharing.ts`, any-ancestor page grants). The path PATCH has no parent rule. (b) *Every tag:* the `create` cap may come from any tag, but each tag must be addable (`canAddTag` = the PATCH `add_tags` rule: create/organize in that tag) OR ungoverned (`tagGoverned`: no grant names it and nothing is published from it) — so `["task","project-x"]` works with create via `task` while `project-x` is nobody's shared folder. (c) *Two notions, do not merge them.* `systemNoteReason` (`@prism/core/pages`) = TRUE system notes: tags `agent-skill`/`agent-dispatch`/`agent-session`/`alert`, anything under `vault/agent`, every `governance-*` record — read-only below workspace admin whatever the grants: PATCH, restore, DELETE, `/notes/:id/meta`, `/properties` + batch, attachment upload → 403, `edit_note` proposals refused, and the collab socket / command endpoint / MCP collab tools see level `view` (`collabLevelFor`/`collabAccess`, keyed on ROLE so an `own` grant on the tag does not lift it). `protectionReason` = the wider PLACEMENT set (system notes + ingest-owned: meetings, ClickUp tasks, people, message threads/archives, `vault/_inbox`, `vault/messages`): those stay EDITABLE per grants (body, metadata, a task's status via `/properties`, comments, live collab, restore, delete) and are pinned only against move / trash / path PATCH, adding or removing a system tag, creating into a system tag, and forking. **Round 3 (independent review).** *Tags are canonicalised ONCE, like the vault* — `apps/server/src/tags.ts` `canonicalTag(raw) = raw.replace(/^[#\s]+/, "").trim()` (the vault's `stripTagHash`: leading `#`/whitespace run stripped, tail trimmed, empties dropped; NO case folding — vault tags are case-sensitive — and no name-based hierarchy, only admin-written `parent_names`). Every tag from a request passes through it BEFORE any check and before it is sent (create, PATCH `add_tags`/`remove_tags`, governance payloads, CSV import + schema tag, and `vaultClient`'s own `sinkTags`, which also refuses an empty tag); `"#agent-skill"` used to pass every check and be stored as `agent-skill`. *Paths are canonical too:* `normalizePagePath` strips trailing `.md` (the vault does — `vault/agent.md` IS `vault/agent`) and NFC-normalises; a non-owner create / path PATCH refuses a path some note already answers to case-insensitively in either Unicode form (`noteAtPath`/`pathKey` — the vault's unique index is binary but its lookups are NOCASE), and the parent page is found the same way (two pages on one key = refused). The path PATCH applies the create's parent rule. *Database rows:* a database page is an allowed parent when the new note carries all of its `prism_database.source.tags` and the actor has `create` in each (view on the page suffices). *Ingest keys* (`INGEST_KEYS` in `api.ts`: `source_id`, `calendarEventId`, `messageId`, `threadId`, `matrixRoomId`, `skillName`, `runner`, `lastRun`, `executionMode`, `merged_into`/`mergedInto`/`superseded_by`, `prism_merge_*`) are dropped from a non-owner create and from a PATCH unless unchanged. *The editor-schema gate* runs only for notes the caller can view. *Skill scheduler defence in depth* (`untrustedSkillReason`, `worker/skills.ts`): an `agent-skill` note runs only under `vault/agent/skills/` AND with `prism_creator` absent or a workspace owner/admin; others are skipped, logged once, counted in `/acl/workers` `skills.untrustedSkills`. *Admins are exempt from the system-note rule* by design (the gateway short-circuits owner+admin to the vault passthrough; `collabLevelFor` keys on `roleAtLeast(role, "admin")`) — a workspace admin can edit skill and governance notes. (d) *No existence oracle:* a note the caller cannot view answers 404 `not_found` exactly like a missing one on GET/PATCH/DELETE/versions/restore and the pages routes (403 only when they can view but lack the cap); the client's `isAccessUnavailable` treats 403/404 alike and the access-request flow never needed the 403. (e) *Governance runs on the vault token, so it checks the caller:* `POST /fork`, `/forks/:id/propose-merge` and `edit_note` proposals (both `/content/propose` and the generic `/proposals`) need a strict note id the caller can VIEW (404 otherwise) and refuse system notes; a fork is private to its forker (`prism_creator` + `prism_visibility: private`, reserved metadata not inherited, path dropped in exported/protected locations).

**Tree projection (`GET /api/tree`, `src/tree.ts`, WP7.1).** The file tree no longer lists the vault: the server holds a per-vault in-memory projection of `[{id, path, tags, updatedAt, type?, prismType?}]` (`type` = `metadata.type`, `prismType` = `metadata.prism_type` — the only two metadata keys `inferContentType`/ProjectTree read; creator/visibility are kept internally for the private-note rule but never emitted). Served to owners (vault chosen by `X-Prism-Vault`) AND non-owners (filtered per request through `capsFor(...).has("view")`, so no path/tag of an unviewable note leaks); registered BEFORE the owner short-circuit; gzip via `hono/compress`; `ETag` (weak, content hash) + `If-None-Match` → 304, `Cache-Control: private, no-cache`. **Freshness, three layers:** (1) the vault's live subscribe WebSocket (`/vault/<v>/api/subscribe?include_content=false`, first-message auth with the server's vault token) — its *snapshot is the build* (no separate REST list), `upsert`/`remove` frames keep it current for writes made anywhere; reconnect with backoff 2→60 s, each reconnect snapshot replaces the rows wholesale; (2) write-through from the gateway (owner passthrough via `treeAfterOwnerWrite`, non-owner create/patch/restore/delete) so the writer's next read is exact; older `updatedAt` never clobbers a newer row; (3) fallback: socket failing / no snapshot within `TREE_SNAPSHOT_TIMEOUT_MS` (60 s) → ONE lean list (`include_content=false&include_metadata=type,prism_type,prism_creator,prism_visibility`), plus a `TREE_REBUILD_MS` (5 min) periodic rebuild while the socket isn't live; broad changes (`/tags*` writes, batch verbs) debounce a rebuild. Built lazily on first request per vault (first load waits for the snapshot; later loads are memory). Env: `TREE_SUBSCRIBE=0` disables the socket (tests do), `PRISM_TREE_LOG=1` logs rows/build ms/served bytes. Web `rest.listTree` uses it (falls back to the legacy full list on an older server's 404/403) and the IDB read cache now caches it (under the 4 MB cap; ~2.4 MB raw / ~0.4 MB gzip at 14k notes, estimated). **Desktop still uses its Rust `vault_list_tree`** (it talks to the vault directly, no gateway).

**Invalidation channel (`GET /api/events`, `src/events.ts`, WP7.2, `docs/events.md`).** SSE, ids only: `{type:"note",id,op:"upsert"|"remove"}` / `{type:"resync"}`; `: ping` every 25 s. Fed by the SAME per-vault subscribe socket as the tree projection (`tree.ts` exposes `subscribeTreeChanges(entry, listener)` — a per-vault change emitter fired by `upsertRow`/`removeRow`/snapshot replace, so gateway write-through emits too; there is no second socket). **Leak-proof per connection:** every event goes through the actor's `capsFor(...).has("view")` on the row's state BEFORE and AFTER the change — upsert emitted iff viewable before OR after, remove iff viewable just before; a never-viewable note (incl. someone else's private note) yields no frame, because even an id is a leak. Owner/admin: all events for the vault chosen by `X-Prism-Vault`. Capability links: allowed, view-scoped the same way. Anon: 401. Connections are recycled every `EVENTS_MAX_AGE_MS` (15 min) so grants/sessions are re-resolved; capped `EVENTS_MAX_PER_USER` (8) / `EVENTS_MAX_TOTAL` (200) → 429; per-connection queue `EVENTS_BUFFER` (200) overflow → one `resync`. No Last-Event-ID: a reconnect makes the client resync. **Client:** `packages/core/src/lib/events/invalidation.ts` (pure: batches 500 ms → `queryKeys.vault.note(id)`, note LISTS only (never other notes' per-id keys), search/tags/stats/graph + `EXTRA_LIVE_KEYS` matrix rooms / agent dispatches+skills; resync or >50 ids → `vault.all`), `data/InvalidationContext.tsx` (`InvalidationSubscriber`, mounted once inside App's QueryClientProvider; re-opens on `prism:vault-changed`), web transport `apps/web/src/events/httpInvalidationSource.ts` (`streamServerSSE`). Desktop provides no source → no channel, polling unchanged. **Polling is relaxed, not removed:** `useLivePollMs(ms)` returns the old interval while the channel is down and ≥5 min while it is live (Inbox, MessagesDashboard, VaultMessagesDashboard, AgentActivity, StatusBar, service status). Check: `npm run verify:events -w @prism/web`; measure: `apps/server/scripts/measure-idle-clients.ts`.

**Web client.** `apps/web` talks to the gateway with `credentials: "include"` (the session cookie); it stores **no token** (verified: `document.cookie` empty, nothing in localStorage). `main.tsx` gates the app on `GET /auth/me` → `LoginScreen` when unauthenticated.

**Web transport + native build (WP2.2).** ALL server calls in `apps/web` go through `serverFetch` in `apps/web/src/transport.ts` (and `@prism/core`'s governance/review clients through the `setServerFetch` seam, `packages/core/src/lib/transport/serverFetch.ts`) — never a bare `fetch()` (`npm run verify:native -w @prism/web` fails on one). PWA mode = same-origin + `credentials:"include"`; `npm run build:native -w @prism/web` (`--mode native`, output `dist-native/`, no PWA plugin/SW) targets a Tauri shell: origin + device bearer token from `window.__PRISM_HOST__` (`apiOrigin`, `getToken()`, `onUnauthorized()`, `signIn()`), `credentials:"omit"`, the bearer only ever sent to the configured origin, `NativeSignInScreen` instead of `LoginScreen`. `EventSource` can't send headers — use `streamSSE`/`streamServerSSE` (fetch + Last-Event-ID reconnect). Collab provider `token` = device token in native (`collabToken()`). `offline/readCache.ts` is an IndexedDB network-first read-through cache for note/list/tag GETs in both modes (LRU 300 entries/64MB, cleared on sign-out/401/account change). Contract + details: `docs/native-auth.md` § Client transport. A new `/api`-reaching call MUST use `serverFetch` or native mode silently loses auth.

**Gotchas.**
- The PWA service worker's `navigateFallback: index.html` will **shadow server routes** unless they're in `navigateFallbackDenylist` (currently `[/^\/auth\//, /^\/api\//, /^\/health(\?|$)/, /^\/mcp(\/|$)/, /^\/\.well-known\//]` — Workbox tests an entry against pathname + SEARCH, so `/health?live=1` needs the `(\?|$)` form; `check:sw` evaluates the real entries). Any new server-owned route must be added there, or sign-in/API breaks **only in the browser** (curl still works). **New public/server routes must stay under `/api/*` (already denylisted) or be added to the denylist.** `/p/:slug` is intentionally a **CLIENT** route (the SPA shell) while its data is `/api/p/*` — that's why publishing JSON lives under `/api`. Guard: `apps/web/scripts/check-sw-denylist.mjs` (`npm run check:sw -w @prism/web`) asserts every server prefix is denylisted.
- **`GET /health` is bounded; `GET /health?live=1` is the watchdog probe.** `vault.health()` aborts after `VAULT_HEALTH_TIMEOUT_MS` (2000; 0 = no timeout), so a slow/paged-out vault is a prompt `503 {ok:false, vault:false}` instead of a hung request (also bounds `/api/health`). `?live=1` answers `200 {ok:true, live:true}` with NO vault call — process liveness only; point anything that restarts THIS process at it, and keep the plain form for "is the vault reachable". **External watchdogs (pm2/launchd/uptime monitors that restart the server) should probe `/health?live=1`** — the plain form answers 503 whenever the VAULT is slow, which is not a reason to restart Prism. SW denylist entry: `/^\/health(\?|$)/`. Tests: `test/app.test.ts`.
- Run config: `apps/server/.env` (gitignored) — `PARACHUTE_TOKEN`, `SESSION_SECRET`, `CAPABILITY_SECRET`, `OWNER_EMAIL`, optional `RESEND_API_KEY`/`MAGIC_FROM`, `APP_ORIGIN`, `WEB_ROOT` (default `../web/dist`), `FEDERATION_ENABLED` (default off), `GOVERNANCE_SIGNING_SECRET` (governance integrity; unset = off, see governance section), `MEMBER_VAULT_TOKENS` (default off = member whole-vault MCP minting frozen). `assertConfig()` fails fast on missing required secrets.
- **Semantic search / RAG is PRIMARY-VAULT ONLY.** The `embeddings` table has no vault column and is fed only from the primary vault, so every `routes/rag.ts` route (`/api/search/semantic`, `/api/index/*`) returns **409 `semantic_index_primary_only`** for an actor bound to any other vault (`X-Prism-Vault`, or a capability link whose grant lives elsewhere) — even the owner. Answering from the primary index would leak (grants match by tag NAME, not vault) and let a vault-B admin write the primary index. The client (`useVaultSearch`) falls back to full-text on the refusal. A per-vault index is a deferred schema migration.
- Token rotation: `parachute auth revoke-token <jti>` has a **~60s cache TTL** before the vault enforces it. The macOS desktop config is `~/Library/Application Support/prism/prism-config.json` (NOT `~/.config/prism`).

**Publishing (public Wikis).** VAULT-AWARE as of 2026-09: publications carry `vault_id` (row + backing anyone-grant + the whole anon path via `vaultClient`), resolved from `X-Prism-Vault` at publish time — one publication per (vault, tag); the wiki template has a lazy **Map tab** fed by leak-proof `GET /api/p/:slug/map` (publication-scoped geometry only). `acl.ts` `POST /acl/tags/:tag/publish` (owner; Share dialog Publish tab) turns a **tag** into a public read-only site: it upserts a `publications` row AND an `anyone/tag/view` grant (config + access decoupled). The human URL is `/p/:slug` (SPA); data is `/api/p/*` in `publish.ts` (mounted before the gateway, so it never hits the owner short-circuit / 403). The public path synthesizes an **anon actor** whose grants are the tag's `anyone` grants — `effectiveLevel` stays the only guard, tag just narrows. Publishing a tag is **dynamic** (future-tagged notes auto-appear). Graph/wikilinks are leak-proofed to the in-set notes only (out-of-set edges dropped). Optional password: scrypt hash + httpOnly `pub_<slug>` HMAC unlock cookie (`POST /api/p/:slug/auth`); a locked manifest withholds the nav. **Password sites REQUIRE https** (the unlock cookie is `secure` only when `APP_ORIGIN` is https). Unpublish (`DELETE`) removes both the row and the `anyone` grant. Docs: `docs/publishing.md`.

**Parachute-to-Parachute federation (gated).** Off unless `FEDERATION_ENABLED=true`; `federation-manager.ts` is imported lazily so the default deploy never loads the `@hocuspocus/provider` client. Trust: Ed25519 pairing — owner mints a one-time code (`POST /acl/peers/pair`), peer hub redeems it (`POST /api/federation/pair`) registering its pubkey. **Spaces** group notes; `POST /acl/spaces/:id/notes` mints a content-independent `space_note_key` (both hubs share it) and **pins the collab kind**. Peers get `subject_type=peer / space` grants; `effectiveLevel` extends via `NoteRef.spaceIds`. One-doc model: each hub serves a Y.Doc under `space_note_key` and bridges a provider to the peer's `/collab` (same doc id → no double-write/echo). **Suggest** level → durable `pending_suggestions` inbox (`/acl/suggestions`, survives restart). **Honest deferred gaps** (in `federation-manager.ts` header): no peer-URL registry (`syncSpaces()` takes URLs from caller), clients don't yet open federated notes by `space_note_key`, and two-hub convergence needs a 2nd live hub+vault. Docs: `docs/federation.md`.

**Onboarding & setup (prism setup / plugin).** `node --import tsx apps/server/scripts/prism-setup.ts` (`--dry-run`/`--force`) generates secrets, writes `.env` (chmod 600), and idempotently seeds vault tag schemas via `seedTagSchemas()` (source of truth: `packages/core/src/lib/schemas/tag-schemas.json`; seeds **description + fields** only, additive, never overwrites). The `prism-setup` Claude skill drives the same seed. Auth is invite-only: owner bootstraps via owner-only magic link, everyone else via invite → password register. The web shell **skips the Tauri-only onboarding wizard by default** (`main.tsx` sets `skipOnboarding={isViewer}`, `isViewer=true`); only a genuine owner with `VITE_WEB_OWNER_ONBOARDING=true` sees it. Docs: `docs/onboarding.md`.

**Push notifications (WP3.3, `docs/push.md`).** Agent turn end (`done|error|interrupted`) → `notifyTurnEnd()` (`apps/server/src/push.ts`, called from `agent-sessions.ts`, fire-and-forget) → Web Push via `web-push` to the owner's `push_subscriptions`. Payload is **ids only**; notification text is generic. Routes `/api/push/*` were server-owner-only; since wave 2A any signed-in user registers their OWN browser/device (inbox notifications), while agent-turn pushes still only reach the session owner. VAPID keys in `.env` (`npm run gen-vapid -w @prism/server`); unset = disabled. SW stays `generateSW`: handlers are in `apps/web/public/push-sw.js` via Workbox `importScripts` (keep `push-sw.js` in `globIgnores`). Click opens `/agent/<id>` (client route) → `apps/web/src/push/deeplink.ts` hands the id to the Agent view. iOS needs an installed PWA. Native build gets no web push. **APNs (iOS app):** the same `notifyTurnEnd()` also fans out via `src/apns.ts` (ES256 provider JWT via `crypto.sign`, HTTP/2, injectable transport) to `apns_tokens` rows registered by `POST/DELETE /api/push/apns` (+ `/apns/test`) — a signed-in user's `pd_` device credential required (cookie → 403 `device_token_required`; owner-only before wave 2A), one row per device, deleted by `revokeDevice`; `APNS_KEY_PATH` (.p8, must be 0600 or APNs stays off) / `APNS_KEY_ID` / `APNS_TEAM_ID` / `APNS_TOPIC` are `.env`-only host config, unset = off; TestFlight tokens are PRODUCTION; contract in `docs/push.md` § APNs.

**Credentials are write-only everywhere (`docs/credentials.md` = the full table).** No frontend ever receives a stored credential; it gets `configured` (+ non-secret scope fields) and can replace/remove. **Server integrations** (`/api/integrations/<kind>`, `SECRETS_KEY`-encrypted, per vault): GET never echoes a secret; set in Network → Server (web, Prism Client via device token, desktop via the `api_request` proxy). `proton-bridge` is server-owner only; `POST /api/integrations/proton-bridge/detect-cert` reads the LOOPBACK listener's cert (greeting → `A1 STARTTLS` → handshake → close; non-loopback refused before any socket; never loads or sends a credential; same `certFingerprintOf` as the login pin) and the UI requires an explicit "this is my Bridge" confirmation before saving a detected pin (deliberate TOFU). **Owner-added vault tokens** rotate via `PUT /acl/vaults/:id/token` (server-owner, probed first, never echoed; env vaults → 400 `env_configured`). **Host bootstrap secrets** (`SESSION_SECRET`, `CAPABILITY_SECRET`, `SECRETS_KEY`, `PARACHUTE_TOKEN`/`PRISM_VAULTS`, `COLLAB_TOKEN`, `GOVERNANCE_SIGNING_SECRET`, `PEER_SIGNING_KEY`, VAPID, `RESEND_API_KEY`, `EMBED_API_KEY`) are `.env`-only — never add one to `EDITABLE_ENV` (`apps/server/src/env-edit.ts`, used by `PUT /acl/server/config`): a stolen owner session must not be able to rotate the root of trust or re-route sign-in. `RESEND_API_KEY` and `APP_ORIGIN` were removed (they carry/build owner magic links); only `MAGIC_FROM` remains, behind the central `isSafeEnvValue` guard (no CR/LF/NUL/control chars — a newline used to inject `OWNER_EMAIL=` lines) + a strict address pattern + a function replacer; tests parse the result with `util.parseEnv`. Desktop `AppConfig::load_from` is STRICT (a corrupt file is an error, never the first-launch save of defaults) and `save_to` is atomic (0600 temp + fsync + rename; a corrupt original is kept as `.corrupt-<ts>`); the `/api` proxy path must pass a `[A-Za-z0-9_/-]` allowlist AND parse (url crate) to itself under `/api/integrations`. **Desktop** `get_full_config` returns `""` + `<key>_set` for every `SECRET_FIELDS` entry (`redacted_config_view`); `update_config` merges (`apply_config_updates`: value replaces, blank keeps, `null` clears; clearing `anthropic_api_key` also deletes its Keychain item) and now starts from the ON-DISK config (the launch-time managed state used to make a second save revert the first). Only `get_collab_config` hands `collab_token` to the webview (the collab WS is opened from JS) — never rendered.

## Real-time Collaboration (type-aware Hocuspocus)

`apps/server/src/collab.ts` runs a **Hocuspocus (Yjs) WebSocket** at `/collab`. It is **type-aware**: `noteKind(note)` → `document | code | spreadsheet | canvas` (by `metadata.prism_type` → tag → file extension), and `loadDocumentState`/`storeDocumentState` (plus the "already populated" guard) seed and persist the matching Yjs structure ⇄ the note's canonical content. The server self-seeds from Parachute, so a shared doc opens even with the owner offline. Per note, the live Yjs binary is cached in SQLite (`collab_docs`) for CRDT continuity; an external Parachute edit (vault `updatedAt` newer than our snapshot) wins and re-seeds.

| Kind | Yjs structure (field) | Persisted as | Client editor (`@prism/core`) |
|---|---|---|---|
| document | `XmlFragment` ("default") | HTML | `CollabEditor` (TipTap) — + comments + suggestions |
| code | `Y.Text` ("codemirror") | raw source | `CollabCodeEditor` (CodeMirror + y-codemirror.next) |
| spreadsheet | `Y.Array<Y.Array<string>>` ("rows") | CSV | `CollabSpreadsheet` (grid) |
| canvas | `Y.Map<id, element>` ("elements") | Excalidraw scene JSON | `CollabCanvas` (Excalidraw) |

Connection auth (`authorizeConnection`) resolves the same `effectiveLevel`: below `view` → reject; below **`edit`** → read-only (**suggest-only enforcement**: raw Yjs updates need edit; Hocuspocus refuses a read-only connection's `Update`/`SyncStep2` wholesale, and the client sees `provider.authorizedScope === "readonly"`). Suggest-level people and capability guests keep live reading + presence and change a prose document only through the bounded, server-authored commands at `POST /api/collab/:id/commands` (`src/human-collab.ts` + `routes/human-collab.ts`: suggested insert/delete/replace within one paragraph, comment, reply, resolve, delete-comment; server-derived identity; revision + quote checks → 409; durable SQLite receipts `collab_command_receipts`; per-actor rate (`COLLAB_COMMANDS_PER_MINUTE`, 30) and size budgets; code/sheet/canvas → 400). A collab document is named by a note **id** only — `resolveLevel` and the endpoint refuse a path/title alias the vault would resolve (it would open a second Y.Doc for the same note). `COLLAB_SUGGEST_ENFORCED=false` restores the old writable suggest socket (rollback switch; the endpoint works either way). **Release order: server with the flag `false` → updated client → flag on** — the pre-enforcement client lets a suggest user type edits the server then refuses. **The client is activated (wave 2D):** `collabAffordances(level, provider.authorizedScope)` gives a suggest person on a read-only socket `commands: true` and a NON-editable body; `HumanSuggestionComposer` (bubble "Suggest edit": replace/delete/insert before/after in one paragraph, single line; "Comment") and `CommentsSidebar actions` (reply/resolve/delete) send commands via `apps/web/src/collab/humanCommands.ts` `sendHumanCommand` (cookie/device bearer, `?t=` for links, never auto-retried; Retry resends the SAME request; 409 keeps the draft and asks for the passage again; ids trusted only from a 200). The revision hashes `editor.state.doc.toJSON()`, which must equal the shared-schema projection of the fragment (verified equal in real Chromium + WebKit against the real server, plain + rich docs); if they differ a suggestion/comment capture is REFUSED (positions could address other text), while thread actions (reply/resolve/delete, no positions) hash the projection. Sidebar thread actions keep one pending command per (action, thread, payload) and resend it unchanged after an outcome-unknown failure. The "trusted collaborators" disclosure is gone, so **production MUST run with `COLLAB_SUGGEST_ENFORCED=true` (or unset) once this client is deployed** — PWA first, then the Prism Client rebuild. Real-server browser journeys: `apps/web/e2e-fixtures/suggest-only.spec.ts` (+ `real-server.ts`, which runs `apps/server/test/fixtures/e2e-server.ts`: the actual app + collab over the fake vault and in-memory SQLite). Contract + limits: `docs/roadmap/workspace-experience/BACKEND-STATUS.md`; tests `test/suggest-enforcement.test.ts`, `test/human-collab.test.ts`. **Comments need `suggest`** (WP0.2): a comment thread is a Y.Doc write (anchor = `comment` mark in the body, data = `comments` Y.Map), so a `comment`-level actor can write one neither over the socket nor through the command endpoint. A comments-map-only server filter was rejected as unsafe (the anchor is a body write, and dropping one of a client's updates leaves a Yjs clock gap so its later updates pend forever); instead `@prism/core` `collabAffordances(level)` (also `@prism/core/collab-access`) gives `view`/`comment` users NO write affordances, pinned live in `test/comment-level.test.ts`. Hocuspocus 4.1 leaks a never-destroyed scratch `Awareness` (a live `setInterval`) per inbound awareness message — live-socket tests pass `awareness: null` and `unloadDocument` every doc or the test process never exits. Comments + suggested edits are **document-only**; code/sheet/canvas are pure collaborative data. `apps/web/src/collab/CollabDoc.tsx` mirrors `detectKind` and routes to the right editor; `Canvas.tsx`'s `COLLAB_TYPES` set drives the **in-app** live-editor swap (the owner's main app shows the live collaborative editor for any shared note of these kinds). 98 server tests (`test/collab.test.ts`) cover kind detection + per-kind round-trip + corruption guards.

**Editor schema handshake (block editor, `COLLAB_SCHEMA_VERSION = 5` — v3 = the `mention` node (wave 2A), v4 = media/embed/bookmark/TOC/database blocks + image align/caption (wave 2B), v5 = `childPage`, toggle `level`, column `width` + up to 5 columns, table `cellColor` (wave 4A); `packages/core/src/editor/collabSchema.ts`).** y-prosemirror DELETES nodes/marks its schema can't represent, so a stale client would destroy new blocks (callout/toggle/columns/colours/tables/images) for everyone. Live editors connect to `/collab?schema=2`; `onAuthenticate` refuses a DOCUMENT socket with a missing/older `schema` (after authorization) with reason `update_required: …` → client shows "Update required — Reload". `serverFetch` sends `X-Prism-Editor-Schema: 2`; `routes/api.ts` returns 409 `editor_update_required` for a content PATCH/PUT without it ONLY when the stored note has v2+ markers (callout/toggle/columns/colours, and since v3 `data-type="mention"`) (owner passthrough included; in-process MCP exempt). Any node/mark/attr change MUST bump the version (`test/collab-schema-gate.test.ts` pins names). **Release order: server (pm2 restart) → PWA → rebuild/reinstall Prism Client; never edit with the legacy desktop afterwards** (it talks to the vault directly and can't be gated). Toggle open/closed is view state, not a document attribute. Docs: `docs/client-app.md` § Editor schema handshake.

**Media, embeds and attachments (wave 2B, schema v4).** Blocks in `packages/core/src/editor/blocks.ts` (isomorphic; the server seeds/persists them): `attachment` (`<div data-type="attachment" data-kind="file|pdf|audio|video" data-src data-name data-size data-mime><a href>name</a></div>`), `embed` (stores ONLY `data-url` (+ `data-height`); the iframe src is DERIVED at view time by `lib/media/embeds.ts` `embedFor`), `bookmark` (`data-url/-title/-description/-site/-image/-favicon`, from `GET /api/unfurl`), `tableOfContents` (`<div data-type="toc">`), `databaseView` (inline/linked database: `<div data-prism-database="<noteId>" data-view="<viewId>">`, strict id shapes, React node view `lib/tiptap/databaseView.tsx` → 2C's `renderDatabaseBlock`; slash items Table/Board/Gallery/List/Calendar (new database as a sub-page, `createInlineDatabase`) + "Linked view of database" (`addLinkedView`) through `InsertDatabaseDialog`), image `align`/`caption`, and `codeBlock` = CodeBlockLowlight (same name/`language` attr; highlighting is decoration-only; Tab indents). Node VIEWS are browser-only (`lib/tiptap/mediaViews.ts`, registered into the schema's `registerBlockViews` at import; the server never imports them). **Node-view gotcha:** never write an attribute on a view's `contentDOM` unless it changed, and ignore your own attribute mutations — ProseMirror answers an un-ignored mutation with a redraw that destroys the view.
- **URL rules (security review H1/H2; `lib/media/attachments.ts`).** An attachment's `data-src` is `safeAttachmentSrc`: our own `/api/attachments/<id>` or an https URL — and ONLY an own attachment is ever framed (PDF) or played (audio/video); an https "pdf"/"video" is a plain card whose button opens a new tab (never `serverFetch`). `data-kind` is independent of `data-src`, so the view decides by `isOwnAttachment`, never by kind. No other same-origin path is accepted anywhere. Bookmark `image`/`favicon` = `ownOrProxiedSrc` (own attachment or the server's `/api/media/proxy?u=…` path — never a raw third-party URL in a shared doc). Covers = `safeMediaSrc` (own, proxied, or an http(s) URL the user chose). **Images are never dropped on parse** except `javascript:`/`vbscript:`/`data:` (`isDangerousImageSrc`): relative Markdown images, `//cdn`, `cid:`, `blob:` survive a live open/autosave (deleting the node deleted the image for good); the view simply sets what the browser can load. A block whose URL fails is not matched, so its fallback `<a>` survives as a paragraph link. Tests: `apps/server/test/editor-blocks-v4.test.ts`, `notion-media.spec.ts` (H1/H2).
- **Embeds.** `EMBED_FRAME_SOURCES` (path-scoped CSP sources) = YouTube-nocookie `/embed/`, Vimeo `/video/`, Loom `/embed/`, Figma `/embed`, Google Docs `/document/` `/spreadsheets/` `/presentation/` (NOT Forms), Google Maps `/maps/embed`, Spotify `/embed/`, X `platform.twitter.com/embed/`; CodePen removed (arbitrary user script). `isAllowedFrameSrc` enforces the same origin+path rule before a frame exists; anything else renders as a bookmark card, never a blank frame. Sandbox = `allow-scripts allow-same-origin allow-popups allow-presentation` — no top-navigation, no forms, no downloads, and no `allow-popups-to-escape-sandbox` (no listed provider needs it; the block's "Open in …" link is the unsandboxed way out). **Web CSP (`app.ts`, pinned in `test/app.test.ts`):** `frame-src 'self' https://www.youtube-nocookie.com/embed/ https://player.vimeo.com/video/ https://www.loom.com/embed/ https://www.figma.com/embed https://docs.google.com/document/ https://docs.google.com/spreadsheets/ https://docs.google.com/presentation/ https://www.google.com/maps/embed https://open.spotify.com/embed/ https://platform.twitter.com/embed/` and `media-src 'self' blob:`. **Prism Client CSP is UNCHANGED (owner decision pending):** in the native build an embed is an "Open in <provider>" card and a PDF a download card (`frameAllowedHere` reads `window.__PRISM_HOST__.frameOrigins`, which no host sets today). The reviewer's minimal safe set for a later decision: `youtube-nocookie.com`, `player.vimeo.com`, optionally Spotify/Loom — with no popups.
- **Paste / find.** A bare URL paste → link + "Paste as" menu (Mention/URL/Bookmark/Embed; `UrlPaste.ts`, `PasteUrlMenu.tsx`). Find & replace (`EditorFindBar`, ⌘F / **⌘⌥F** — ⌘⇧H is highlight since wave 4A; `lib/tiptap/findShortcuts.ts`, which also carries the `prism:find-in-page` window event the page ⋯ menu fires for phones): matches use ORIGINAL-text offsets (case-insensitive regex, no `toLowerCase()` drift), never touch a `[[wikilink]]` span or an atom, an empty replacement deletes exactly the match (`tr.delete`, never `deleteRange`), replace-all is ONE transaction (one undo step, one Yjs update; works in live docs) and is offered only when `structuralEditsAllowed`.
- **Covers + files.** Page cover (`PageCover.tsx`): metadata `cover` = `gradient:<name>` | image URL, `coverY` 0–100; `PageHeader` gains `onAddCover` and a named **`presence` slot**. `coverForNote(row)` feeds database gallery cards (page cover image with its focal point, a gradient, or the first image of the view's `coverKey` property; `coverY` rides in the query row meta). **Files & media property** (`kind: "files"`, vault type `array`): values are `[name](/api/attachments/<id>)` links (`fileRef`/`parseFileRefs`); `PropertyValue` takes `noteId` and uploads to the row's page (`uploadAttachment(noteId, file, {kind:"file"})`), image thumbnails, download on click.
- **Attachments (`routes/attachments.ts` + `src/attachments.ts`, mounted in `api.ts` before the owner passthrough).** Storage is the VAULT (`POST /storage/upload` under a server-chosen `upload.<ext>` + `POST /notes/:id/attachments {path,mimeType,transcribe:false}`), so vault backups include the bytes; Prism indexes them in SQLite `prism_attachments(id a_<22>, vault_id, note_id, storage_path, mime, size, name, created_by, created_at, status live|orphan_*|deleted, vault_attachment_id, deleted_at, flagged_at)`. `POST /api/notes/:id/attachments[?kind=image|file]` (multipart `file`; user or capability link with `edit`; MCP 403; CSRF = multipart + REQUIRED `X-Prism-Upload: 1` + Sec-Fetch-Site/Origin unless device token; `ATTACHMENT_MAX_BYTES` 25 MB / images 10 MB / capability links `ATTACHMENT_LINK_MAX_BYTES` 5 MB → 413; rates `ATTACHMENT_UPLOADS_PER_MINUTE` 30, links 6; quotas `ATTACHMENT_NOTE_QUOTA_BYTES` 250 MB / `ATTACHMENT_VAULT_QUOTA_BYTES` 5 GB → 413 `quota_exceeded {scope}`; `ATTACHMENT_MAX_CONCURRENT_UPLOADS` 2 (the body is buffered ONCE — the vault only takes multipart) → 503 `busy`; strict id + `note.id === id`; no view → 404, trashed → 404, no edit → 403, locked non-admin → 409; type by MAGIC BYTES — images PNG/JPEG/GIF/WebP/AVIF, files also PDF/MP3 (two consecutive frames)/M4A/OGG/WAV/FLAC/WebM/MP4/MOV else octet-stream; SVG/HTML/XML/script bytes (incl. UTF-16) or extensions → 415; bidi controls stripped from names; the note body is never written) → 201 `{id,url,name,mimeType,size}`. `GET /api/attachments/:id`: role/grants are resolved against the ROW's `vault_id` (elements can't send `X-Prism-Vault`); owning note's VIEW cap (note cached ≤5 s), trashed → 404, anon → 404; one Range forwarded; headers rebuilt (sniffed type, nosniff, CORP same-origin, `Cache-Control: private, no-cache` + `ETag` + `Vary: Authorization, Cookie`; disposition inline for image/audio/video/PDF but ALWAYS `attachment` on `Sec-Fetch-Dest: document`; CSP `default-src 'none'; sandbox`, PDFs `default-src 'none'; frame-ancestors 'self'` + `X-Frame-Options: SAMEORIGIN`). Purge ORDER: a page's attachments are released only AFTER the vault deleted the page (`DELETE /trash/:id` and the trash purge worker, per note) — a failed delete leaves the page restorable with its media. The vault cascades attachment rows with the note but keeps the stored files and cannot unlink them without the note, so those rows become recorded orphans (`orphan_note_deleted`, storage path kept, never served; reclaiming the bytes needs vault-side support); a failed attach after upload is recorded `orphan_attach_failed`. Owner-only `POST /api/attachments/sweep {dryRun=true, limit, cursor}` flags unreferenced rows (`orphan_unreferenced`; bytes are kept — a block can come back from history) and reports recorded orphans (`recorded`, `recordedTotal`, `recordedBytes`; ids + sizes only). Client: `VaultClient.uploadAttachment(noteId, file, {kind})` / `unfurl(url)`; native + capability-link viewers get `blob:` URLs for `/api/attachments/…` (and, native, `/api/media/proxy…`) via `apps/web/src/native/externalImages.ts` (credential in a header, never in a URL). Published pages and copied pages: see *Wave 3 gaps* (`/api/p/:slug/attachments/:id`, `POST /api/notes/:id/attachments/copy`).
- **`GET /api/unfurl?u=`**: signed-in only (links/anon/MCP 401), `UNFURL_PER_MINUTE` 60, the media proxy's SSRF guard AND its in-flight pools (503 `busy`), 1 MB / 8 s, HTML only (415), a linear regex-free head scanner with bounded entity decoding (`media/unfurl-parse.ts`); returns `{url,title,description,siteName,image,favicon}` where image/favicon are `/api/media/proxy?u=…` paths or null.

**Content conversion — NEVER on the main thread (`apps/server/src/convert/`, 2026-10 availability fix).** The server is one process, and every parser of note content is super-linear or simply slow: `marked` 18 (18.0.5 installed) is quadratic on emphasis runs (20 KB of `*a ` ≈ 2 s, 60 KB ≈ 17 s) and overflows its stack on deep blockquotes; turndown and the ProseMirror DOM parser recurse per nesting level (264 KB of nested `<div>` ≈ 5 s, then a stack overflow); and ORDINARY content costs ~4 s/MB in TipTap `generateJSON` and ~0.4 s/MB in `generateHTML` over happy-dom. Any member who could author a note could stall collab, ingest, sign-in and the gateway. **Rule: no server module imports `marked`, `turndown`, `happy-dom` or calls `generateJSON`/`generateHTML` — only `convert/core.ts` (pure conversions, loaded by the worker AND, for small input, the main thread) and the transfer worker; everything else calls `convert/service.ts`** (`test/conversion.test.ts` greps for offenders).
- **Service** (`convert/service.ts`): `markdownToHtml`, `htmlToMarkdown`, `blocksHtmlToMarkdown` (the block-move flavour), `contentToDocJson`, `htmlToDocJson`, `contentToSeed` (note body → Yjs seed), `docJsonToHtml`; each takes `{actor?, lane?}`. A LINEAR pre-check (`convert/precheck.ts`: size, emphasis/link delimiter runs per block, blockquote depth, element depth, node count — one pass, no regex) → only TINY plain input converts INLINE (same code, byte-identical; Markdown AND stored HTML ≤ `CONVERT_INLINE_MAX_BYTES` 24 KB, ≤ `CONVERT_INLINE_MAX_NODES` **250** pieces to parse (round 5 — measured on the event loop: a paragraph ≈ 0.15 ms, a list item 0.25–1 ms, a table cell 0.3–0.5 ms; the old 2500 was 0.4–1 s), ≤ 200 delimiter runs per block, depth ≤ 40, Markdown ≤ `CONVERT_INLINE_MAX_SPECIALS` 600 characters `marked` gives a meaning to (the net under every shape no counter knows); a RENDER is inline up to 10× the node cap but only within the byte cap, text + attribute strings (`docJsonWeight`) — **stored HTML has the byte cap too (round 4, C1)**: it used to be inline up to 2.5 MB "by node count", the count saw only `<`+letter, and happy-dom builds a node per lone `>`, `/>`, `-->`, comment, `<!…>`, `<?…>` and is super-linear in them (100 KB of `>` stalled the loop 3.4 s). `htmlNodeCount` now counts every `<`, every `>` that closes nothing, and attributes (incl. boolean ones, by name)/entities at ¼; the byte cap bounds whatever a counter misses — never raise it for HTML. **Round 5, C-1 — Markdown multiplies:** `marked` pads every table row to the header's width, so ~4C+2R bytes are C×R cells (6 KB = a million `<td>`; it passed the inline gate AND the worker refusal). `markdownComplexity` adds, per blank-line block that holds a delimiter row, 2 × (most `|` on a line) × (its lines) to `nodes` (every `|` counts, escaped or not; pipes in a block without a delimiter row are text — a chat log is not a table), counts backtick runs and e-mail/`://`/`www.` autolinks, and reports `nestDepth` (list markers + `>` on one line, indentation/2) and `specials` for the inline gate only; a Markdown body with any `<` is counted like stored HTML on top (raw HTML blocks reach the DOM parser verbatim). **Round 6 (B1/B2/S5) — the Markdown counters are conservative BOUNDS, no longer a model of `marked`'s blocks** (five reviews each found a line break or blank line the pre-check read differently: a lone `\r` made 6,000 list items one "line"; a tab-only line ended a table for the pre-check and was a row for `marked`): (a) `normalizeLineBreaks` (`\r\n?` → `\n`, in `precheck.ts`) runs ONCE in `convertText`/`assertCheapText`/`conversionRefusal`/`isCheapContent` and again (idempotent) at every text entry of `convert/core.ts`, so the pre-check and the parser — inline or in the worker — read identical bytes; (b) EVERY line is a node (blank ones too) plus one per container its leading run can open (`> > > x` + blank, 120× = lines × depth), and FF / VT / NEL / U+2028 / U+2029 each end a line for the count; (c) table cells are bounded for the DOCUMENT: if a line that could be a delimiter row (only `|`, `-`, `:`, blanks and leading container markers, with a `-` and a `|` or `:`) exists anywhere, `nodes += 2 × (most `|` on any line + 2) × (lines after the first such line + 1)` — no blocks, no blank-line rule; (d) `nestDepth` counts the line's WHOLE leading run (`>`, list markers, every blank — also after a marker; tab = 4 columns); (e) a paragraph/table region ends only at an EMPTY or U+0020-only line (`marked`'s own rule). `Complexity.parseNodes` = the same count with cells bounded per table region (candidate delimiter row → next empty/spaces-only line, width = the region's widest candidate): it decides the up-front `too_many_nodes` refusal, so a long ordinary note with one small table is still convertible (a miss there costs a worker thread, never the event loop). The inline byte cap stays 24 KB — measured worst inline cases are tiny inputs bounded by the node cap (≤ ~30 ms idle, ~130 ms under load). A new shape = add it to `MD_SHAPES`/`HTML_SHAPES` in `test/fixtures/conversion-shapes.ts` (rounds 5 + 6 hold it to: not cheap, or measured inline < 200 ms lag — round 6 with every line-break variant); `test/conversion-round6.test.ts` also asserts the invariant itself on 600 seeded random bodies (elements `marked` produces ≤ 2 × counted `nodes`; inline conversion < 200 ms). **Never run a hostile shape inline against an older pre-check.** **Round 6 follow-up — two more inline-gate bounds the widened property test found** (`marked` looks AHEAD from a delimiter): `scanCost` = most (delimiter runs in a block × that block's length) ≤ `CONVERT_INLINE_MAX_SCAN` 300,000 (200 `[a](` before a 23 KB tail was 0.65 s), and `blankRun` = the longest run of whitespace of ANY kind ≤ `CONVERT_INLINE_MAX_BLANK_RUN` 256, Markdown and stored HTML (the link rule backtracks over `\s` runs: 61 `[a](` + 4 KB of NBSP — 4.5 KB in all — was **15.8 s** inline). `parseNodes` counts a line as `1 + its `>`/list markers`, NOT its indentation (10,000 lines of indented JSON are 10,000 lines; indentation stays in `nodes`/`nestDepth`).) → everything else runs in the conversion worker (`transfer/worker.ts` ops `md-html`/`html-md`/`doc-json`/`doc-seed`/`doc-html`; `TaskWorker` with `workerData {preload:"doc"}` so a task's clock never includes module load) under a wall clock of `CONVERT_TIMEOUT_MS` 8 s + `CONVERT_TIMEOUT_PER_MB_MS` 30 s/MB (max `CONVERT_TIMEOUT_MAX_MS` 60 s) — past it the thread is TERMINATED and respawned. Failure = `ConversionError(reason)`; **the caller owns a deterministic, safe fallback**:
  - `too_large` / `too_complex` / `too_many_nodes` = the PRE-CHECK refused the input (`isDeterministicFailure`): `CONVERT_MAX_BYTES` 2.5 MB, `CONVERT_MAX_DELIMITERS` 20k runs in one block, `CONVERT_MAX_QUOTE_DEPTH` 200, `CONVERT_MAX_INPUT_NODES` (lines + delimiter runs + HTML pieces) and `CONVERT_MAX_NODES` (a render) — **both default from the heap**: 100 resp. 200 nodes per MB of `CONVERT_HEAP_MB`, at most 200k / 400k (measured: happy-dom + ProseMirror need ~4–5 KB per node). With the default 512 MB heap that is 51k / 102k: a body beyond it is refused by name up front instead of running a thread out of memory on every open. Ordinary 2 MB prose is ~66k nodes and needs well over 768 MB → such a note opens live only with `CONVERT_HEAP_MB=2048` (then ~7 s); below that it is shown as plain text.
  - `timeout` (load-dependent), `busy` (no slot — says nothing about the input: retry, never fall back), `failed` (worker crash / out of memory / never came up).
  - **Memory only for a REPEATED timeout** (same input twice within `CONVERT_FAILURE_TTL_MS` 10 min) **or a worker killed TWICE** (round 6, S1/S-5: the thread died WHILE running that input — `WorkerFailedError.duringTask` / `ConversionError.killedWorker`; one death may be an earlier task's doing, the second refuses the hash for `CONVERT_KILLER_TTL_MS` 6 h, in memory only); never `busy`, never a thread that did not come up, and pre-check refusals need none.
  - **Lanes + fairness:** `CONVERT_THREADS` 2 (heap `CONVERT_HEAP_MB` **512** each since round 4 — a ceiling, was 2048: this host shares 16 GB with the vault, a VM and a 7 GB model; idle threads exit after `CONVERT_IDLE_MS` 5 min), `CONVERT_MAX_QUEUE` 32 per thread. With ≥ 2 threads the FIRST serves only `lane: "store"` (saving a live document: its render and its clobber-guard fold); one thread → a store goes to the head of the queue. `actor` (account / link key): at most `CONVERT_PER_ACTOR_INFLIGHT` 2 conversions in the worker, `CONVERT_PER_ACTOR_WAITING` 8 more wait in THAT actor's own line, then `busy` — opens, MCP writes, the note resource, block moves and commands pass it. **Circuit breaker, per thread — DEAD workers only (round 5, H-1):** `CONVERT_BREAKER_FAILURES` (4; 0 = off) consecutive tasks that ended with the thread dead (`worker_failed`: crash / out of memory / never came up) → that thread takes no work for `CONVERT_BREAKER_COOLDOWN_MS` (30 s; only a failed TRIAL doubles it, to `…_COOLDOWN_MAX_MS` 10 min); callers get `busy`, nothing is spawned, and what was queued behind the death is answered `busy` at once (`TaskWorker.flush`); after the cool-down ONE trial task, a success closes it. **A TIMEOUT never counts there** — it is charged to the ACTOR key that sent it (account, link, `doc:<name>` for a document's own stores and reconciler folds): `CONVERT_ACTOR_BREAKER_FAILURES` (3) in a row within 10 min → that actor alone is answered `busy` for `CONVERT_ACTOR_COOLDOWN_MS` (30 s, doubling per failed trial to `…_COOLDOWN_MAX_MS`), no thread used. (Four slow notes from one member used to close the lane everyone opens pages through.) **Round 6:** a KILLED worker (died during the actor's task) is a strike too (S1 — it used to cost the actor nothing, so one member could stay the shared breaker's failing trial); and `ConvertOptions.charge: false` = a strike of THIS conversion is not held against the actor — set for every document LOAD (`onLoadDocument`: the opener did not write the stored note; three slow notes used to leave whoever opened them `busy`, S6). Writers stay charged for what they submit (PATCH/MCP/import/block append); stored content is covered by the per-content memory and `unconvertible` — plus a SOFT per-actor load limit (S-4): `CONVERT_LOAD_STRIKES` (6; 0 = off) uncharged strikes within `CONVERT_LOAD_STRIKE_WINDOW_MS` (10 min) → that actor's further uncharged, non-cheap conversions (opens) are `busy` for `CONVERT_LOAD_COOLDOWN_MS` (60 s); its own writes, stores and everyone else are untouched. **Stores use ONLY the first thread (H-2)** — never the idler one, never another when their own is cooling down; a store answered `busy` saves the snapshot ahead and is written later (retry timer / sweep; wait between tries `collabTuning.busyWaitMs`). **A live conversion thread keeps a Node process from exiting** (`.env.test` sets `CONVERT_IDLE_MS=3000`; test files that use the worker call `stopConversionWorkers()` in `after`).
- **Bounded synchronous forms.** `contentToYUpdate`, `yDocToHtml`, `proseToHtml`, `applyExternalContent(doc, kind, content)` without a pre-parsed body, and the `…InHtml` suggestion helpers keep their names for small input (a command's one-block window, tests) but THROW `ConversionError("too_large")` for anything past the inline pre-check — they cannot stall the loop whatever they are handed, so **new server code must not use them on note content** (a live 3,000-paragraph page made `routes/blocks.ts` answer 502).
