- **Editor group (4A) × live updates (4B), settled at integration.** Every block-editor change (block menu commands, drag, paste, Move to's removal, a sub-page row) is a ProseMirror transaction → `onUpdate` → `scheduleSave` → the editor is touched/dirty and saves on its OWN base; nothing in the editor group writes the open page's content around the autosave. "Move to" (`POST /api/notes/:id/blocks/append`) reaches an OPEN plain target as an ordinary remote edit (adopted when clean + idle, offered when not; a dirty target's save 409s into "Needs review"). The route's write is outside the owner proxy, so `api.ts` drops the gateway read cache after it (`api.use("/notes/:id/blocks/*")`) — without that the target's event-driven or next ordinary re-read could be the cached pre-append body; the import's `onWrite` now bumps the write generation too. Pinned by `notion-live-safety.spec.ts` "4A×4B: …" (4 tests; the shell fixture fakes the append route and emits the target's event) and `test/gateway-read-cache.test.ts`.
## Wave 3 gaps (collab routing, unload guard, published/copied attachments, account mentions, swipe)

- **Which editor a suggest-level person gets (in the workspace).** The gateway stamps `_review: "governance"` beside `_caps` (`reviewStamp` in `routes/api.ts`, `governedReview` in `permissions.ts`) only when the caller cannot `edit` AND a grant that matches the note and confers `suggest`/`create` was compiled by governance (`created_by` starts `governance:`). `reviewMode` (`lib/governance/review.ts`): `edit` → `none`; `suggest` + `_review` → `propose` (local draft + Submit for review); a PLAIN suggest share → `read-only` for the plain renderer and `liveSuggest(note)` true — `Canvas`/`RowPeek` only route `propose` away, so that person gets the LIVE suggest-only editor (read-only socket + `/api/collab/:id/commands`), the same one `/collab/:id` gives; `create` without `suggest` → `propose` (unchanged). A hint, never a guard. Release order: server first (an older server sends no stamp → a governed suggester would open the live editor). Tests: `test/review-routing.test.ts`, `notion-suggest-routing.spec.ts`, `suggest-only.spec.ts` (real server, workspace journey).
- **⌘F in read-only / suggest-only live documents.** A non-editable body takes no focus, so `CollabEditor`'s find shortcut also belongs to the editor the reader last clicked or selected in (or the only idle one when nothing has focus and no other editor claimed the key). Find only: `EditorFindBar` hides Replace when `structuralEditsAllowed` is false; ⌘⇧H opens plain find there.
- **Unload guard (live documents, `apps/web/src/collab/localDocument.ts` + `CollabDoc.tsx`).** `persistLocalDocument` tracks the state vector IndexedDB last CONFIRMED. On `pagehide` / `visibilitychange→hidden`, when the socket is down or holds unsynced edits, `rescue()` writes the diff since that vector SYNCHRONOUSLY to localStorage `prism:collab-pending:<same scoped key as the IndexedDB row>` (≤ 1.5 M chars; merged with an earlier rescue) and the document is registered as unsynced; the next `persistLocalDocument` for that key (open or background sync) applies it, writes IndexedDB and removes the entry (only the value it folded in). `beforeunload` warns (web only — never in native) when the socket is down with unsynced edits that IndexedDB has not confirmed (or local storage is unavailable). A rescue entry is a DIFF: it only reads as a document on top of its IndexedDB row. Its key is `prism:collab-pending:<scope tag>.<document tag>` (16-hex FNV tags — no account, vault or document name). **Sign-out (review M3):** `config.logout()` deletes the signing-out scope's `prism-collab-v3` rows and rescue entries (`purgeScopeDocuments`); documents registered unsynced are counted in the keep/download/discard leave prompt (`offline/leave.ts`; download includes each one's Yjs state as base64) and removed once the person leaves; an account change (`bindCacheUser` → changed) purges every OTHER scope's synced rows (`purgeOtherScopes`; their unsynced ones stay, like queued writes). Test: `notion-collab-offline.spec.ts`.
- **MCP actor id.** `prism_suggest_edit` marks carry `data-actor-id` = `documentActorId("user:<email>")` (the opaque `h_…` a human suggestion carries) — never the email. `emailForActorId` also resolves the legacy email form in existing documents (real accounts only). MCP comment items never stored an actor id (unchanged).
- **Sign out.** Optional `AccountClient.signOut()`; the web/native shell wires it to `config.logout()` (unsent-changes prompt, `/auth/logout` or device-token revoke + host `onSignedOut`, read-cache + `prism:offline-*`/recent-search clear) then reloads. The native host's `onSignedOut` runs in `finally` (the token is forgotten even when the revoke request fails); when the server was not reached, `sessionStorage prism:signout-unreached` makes the next sign-in screen say so (local sign-out still completes). UI: sidebar footer `AccountMenu` (Settings / Sign out) and Settings → Account. Absent on the legacy desktop.
- **Published attachments: `GET /api/p/:slug/attachments/:id`** (`routes/publish.ts`). Anonymous, publication-scoped: live + unlocked publication (locked → 401 like the note route), per-IP `PUBLIC_ATTACHMENT_READS_PER_MINUTE` (600), row servable AND in the publication's own vault, owning note in the public set by the single-note rule (`inPublication`: tag + `canPublicView`, or path prefix + not private; not excluded, not trashed), AND that note's current body/metadata still references `/api/attachments/<id>`; everything else → one uniform 404. A global in-flight pool (`PUBLIC_ATTACHMENT_MAX_INFLIGHT` 8, wait `PUBLIC_ATTACHMENT_QUEUE_WAIT_MS` 2 s → 503 `busy`; the slot is held until the body is read, cancelled, or `PUBLIC_ATTACHMENT_SLOT_MAX_MS`) bounds anonymous streams. Bytes + headers come from the shared `serveAttachment` (same hardening as `GET /api/attachments/:id`, which still 404s anon). The wiki renderer rewrites exactly `(src|href|data-src|poster)="/api/attachments/<id>"` to it after sanitising (`rewritePublicAttachments`). Tests: `test/publish-attachments.test.ts`, `publication.spec.ts`.
- **Copied pages get their own files: `POST /api/notes/:id/attachments/copy`** (`routes/attachments.ts`; JSON `{}` + `csrfRefusal`; signed-in user with `edit` on the copy — strict id, unviewable/trashed 404, system note 403, locked 409, live doc 409 `live` (re-checked right before the write); MCP 403; `uploadsPerMinute/3`). Every `/api/attachments/<id>` in the copy's body/metadata is CLASSIFIED first (≤ 2000): the copy's own rows are ignored; unknown/other-vault/unviewable-owner rows are left untouched and counted `skipped` (one count, no reason); the rest are candidates. Per call ≤ 50 candidates, `ATTACHMENT_COPY_MAX_BYTES` (100 MB) and `ATTACHMENT_COPY_MAX_MS` (40 s), ONE upload slot per file (released between files). A candidate is re-uploaded through the vault under the copy (new row, quota checked). Quota/size → the reference is rewritten to a fresh id with NO row (`failed`) — the copy never keeps pointing at a file it can never own; a vault error/timeout → the ORIGINAL reference stays and is counted `errors` (retried by the next call). `more` = work still to do (candidates left or errors). One CAS write (`if_updated_at`, never forced; 409 `conflict`). Client: optional `VaultClient.copyAttachments` loops while a round makes progress and `copyFilesNotice` says "Some files were not copied." when `more`/`errors`/`failed` remain; called by page Duplicate and the database bulk Duplicate before the copy opens. Template copies call it too (`NewContentMenu`, database row templates). Test: `test/attachments-copy.test.ts`.
- **Suggestion accepted/declined notifications** (`suggestionsResolved` in `notifications.ts`, fed by the collab store listener's `prevContent → content` and by the review queue's accept/reject in `acl.ts`): a `data-suggestion-id` that left the document notifies the ACCOUNT behind its actor id (`h_…` or legacy email; none → nothing). Outcome (`suggestionOutcome`) is read from the DECODED text of the parsed prev/next documents and only when unambiguous — text ≥ 4 chars, occurring exactly once in the previous document, both halves of a replacement agreeing; anything else is the neutral `suggestion_resolved` ("resolved your suggestion"), never a guess. Skipped when the suggester is among the batch's editors, on editor-less stores, and when they cannot view the page; decider named only for a single-editor batch; dedupe `suggestion:<note>:<id>`; in the per-sender budgets (an actor id on a mark is writable by an edit-level socket); category `comment`.
- **Home → My tasks: `/api/query {assignedToMe: true}`** (`src/my-tasks.ts`). The server resolves the caller: the SERVER OWNER = the identity layer's owner setting (person note, extra emails, aliases); anyone else = live human person notes whose email identity is their account email (lean `person` listing, 60 s cache). A task matches when an `assigned`/`assignee`/`assigneeEmail`/`assignee_email` value is one of their addresses, a `[[wikilink]]`/path to their person note, or that note's name (owner: a configured alias). Account display names are never used. Links → empty. Only narrows viewable rows; the page carries `identity: "person" | "account" | "unset"` — `unset` = the server owner has no owner person note/alias configured: rows are NOT narrowed (the previous list of every open task) and Home shows a one-line hint to set the owner identity, never an empty list. Home falls back to all open tasks when the answer has no `identity` (older server) or the shell has no `queryNotes`. Test: `test/my-tasks.test.ts`, `notion-home.spec.ts`.
- **Mentioning a member with no person note.** `GET /api/mentions/members?q=` (signed-in user; workspace role ≥ member, else `[]`): members with a display name who are not reachable through a person page the caller can view → `{id: "u_<16 hex>" (writer-stamp `writerIdFor`), name}` — never an email. The chip reuses the existing mention attrs (`kind: person`, `id`, `label`) — **no schema change, `COLLAB_SCHEMA_VERSION` stays 4**. `noteContentStored`/`commentsStored` resolve `u_…` to the account and notify only when EVERY author of the batch is a workspace member (no per-chip attribution — a guest co-editor cannot piggyback) and the recipient (also a member) can view the page; no backlink is written for it. Client: `isAccountMentionId` (plain chip, no profile card), `notificationsApi.mentionMembers`.
- **Swipe actions (`lib/gestures/useSwipeActions`).** Touch events only: inbox rows — left = archive / move to inbox, right = mark read; page-tree rows (phone) — right = (un)favorite, left = the actions sheet. Arms at 72 px; never starts ≤ 24 px from the left edge (shell back/Browse swipe), inside a sideways scroller, on a text field, or for a mostly vertical drag (`touch-action: pan-y`). Reduced motion: no slide, the label shows in place. Every action stays available as a button/menu item. Test: `notion-swipe.spec.ts`.
- **Accepted residuals (wave-3 gaps review).** An `actorId` on a suggestion mark is writable by any edit-level socket, so an editor can make "resolved your suggestion" reach an account that can view the page (bounded by the per-sender budgets). Documents written before the MCP actor-id change keep the email in `data-actor-id` (read, never rewritten). A member with no display name cannot be mentioned by account. The lean `person` listing behind My tasks / member mentions is one vault call per vault per 60 s.

## Wave 8 gaps (title rename, created-by filter, link card, phone gestures)

- **Title rename = the tree's Rename (NP-PG-03).** `renamePageFromTitle(client, {id, path}, newName, onChanged?)` (`lib/pages/titleRename.ts`, plain function — the share route has no providers) → `flushPendingSaves(id)` (the body's debounced autosave goes FIRST, so the move never conflicts with it) → fresh read → `ops.movePage` (`POST /api/notes/:id/move`; per-note path writes only where the shell has no pages routes, the legacy desktop — sub-pages included). Called by `DocumentRenderer.handleRename` and `CollabDoc.handleRename` (through `httpVaultClient`); **never a path PATCH from a title** (it left sub-pages behind and the gateway refuses it for non-owners, `move_required`). A live document is named by the note id, so the open Y.Doc is untouched. Outcomes in `EditableTitle`: `TitleRenameRefused` (`revertTitle`) = 403 / 404 / 409 / 423 (`path_conflict` → "A page named “X” already exists here…", else the server's reason) or offline (one `prism:offline-refused` toast) → the title goes BACK with the reason under it (`[data-title-refused]`) — nobody is held in the field; anything else (5xx, `pending_writes`) keeps the typed title with the reason, and then: **one request per attempted title** (a blur with the title that just failed sends nothing; Enter is the retry) and focus returns to the title only after an Enter the person has not moved on from (never after a blur, never once they typed or clicked in the body). A 207 = renamed + `result.finish()` ("Finish move": the pages toast in the workspace, a button in CollabDoc's notice on the share route). Web `pages.movePage` flushes this page's unsent outbox rows first and refuses (409 `pending_writes`) if they remain. `renamePath` / `pageTitle` / the page header keep only a KNOWN file extension (`fileExtension` in `lib/pages/model.ts`: md, csv, excalidraw, code…): "Plan v1.5" is a whole title (it used to show "Plan v1" and rename to "New.5"). Server rule unchanged: a non-admin's move needs `organize` on the group and a parent PAGE they can add to. Fixtures fake the route with `e2e-fixtures/fake-move.ts`. Specs: `parity2-pages.spec.ts`, `notion-offline-writes.spec.ts`, `document-polish.spec.ts`.
- **Search identity filters (NP-SR-04) — the SERVER does both.** `GET /api/search`: `author=<email>|me` = the CREATOR only (`prism_creator`); `editor=me|<email>` = the LAST EDITOR — the route turns the address into the opaque writer-stamp id (`writerIdFor`) and compares it with `prism_last_writer` (signed-in users only; anyone but an admin may only say `me`; a raw `u_…` id from a client is never honoured; links → `[]`). `GET /api/search/filters` → `{filters:["author","editor"], identity}` lets a client tell this server from an older one. Client: "Created by me" → `author=me`, "Edited by me" → `editor=me`, no client-side narrowing (after the 100-row cap it dropped results); `VaultClient.searchFilterSupport?()` / `useSearchIdentityFilters()` decide what `SearchFilterBar` offers — neither select for share-link viewers and shells without the method, no "Edited by" on a server without `/search/filters` (it would ignore `editor=`). Release order: server first. Tests: `test/search.test.ts`, `notion-search.spec.ts`.
- **Link card (NP-ED-18, `components/renderers/LinkCard.tsx`, both editors).** For LINK MARKS only (looked up in the document: never a wikilink, a mention chip, or a bookmark/attachment anchor): hover (280 ms), the caret in a link, or (read-only) keyboard focus on one → address + Open / Edit / Remove; Edit / Remove only when `structuralEditsAllowed` (not read-only, comment-only or suggesting) → otherwise Open / Copy. It never takes focus; ANY document change hides it and cancels one on its way (positions moved); ⌘K with the CARET in a link fires `LINK_CARD_FOCUS_EVENT` and focus moves into the card (Tab / Shift+Tab walk its actions — handled by the card through `lib/a11y/tabWalk.ts` `walkTab`, because Safari's default Tab skips buttons; at either end the key is the browser's — Esc returns; `walkTab` never takes a Tab that belongs to an IME composition, steps over a stop that refuses focus (`<fieldset disabled>`) and consumes the key only once focus moved — `tab-walk.spec.ts`); `.prism-link-card` is in `EDITOR_POPUPS`. 🔒 **A link never navigates the app window** (`lib/tiptap/prismLinks.ts` `linkTarget` → `openLinkTarget`; there is no `location.assign`): `page` (our `/page/<id>`) → `openTab`, or a NEW tab with `pageLink(id)` where no workspace shell is mounted (`#workspace-document` absent: the share route); `anchor` (`#id`) → scroll; `tab` (any other path that RESOLVES to our own origin, `/auth/logout` included) → a new `noopener noreferrer` tab; `external` (http(s) without `user:pass@`, mailto) → the same; `blocked` = everything else, incl. any value with a backslash, a character ≤ 0x20 or DEL (`/\evil.tld`, `/<TAB>/evil.tld` resolve to another origin) — `normalizeLink` (the inline field) applies the same rule. The click handler listens at the WINDOW (capture) and `preventDefault`s link-mark clicks, so the Prism Client's host script (`host.js`: document-capture, takes un-handled http(s) anchors) leaves them alone: a page link stays in the app and an outside link reaches the host once (through `openInNewTab`) — one native confirmation. Editable: a click lands the caret (⌘/Ctrl-click opens); non-editable: it opens.
- **Pasting a Prism page link → page mention.** `pageIdFromUrl(url, appOrigins())`: parsed origin must EQUAL ours (`location.origin`, or the Prism Client's `__PRISM_HOST__.apiOrigin`), no userinfo, no query, path exactly `/page/<MENTION_ID>` — a look-alike host is an ordinary link. `UrlPaste` inserts the existing `mention` node (`kind: page`, id + uid only; **no schema change, `COLLAB_SCHEMA_VERSION` stays 5**) and sets `UrlPasteState.page`; "Paste as" then offers Page mention / URL (`urlInsteadOfMention`). A page the paster cannot view is still a chip ("No access"). Over a selection the URL still links the text. Spec: `editor-links.spec.ts` (fixture `notion-media.html`, plain + `?live`).
- **Phone gestures (NP-MB-06).** `lib/gestures/usePullToRefresh({onRefresh, label, disabled})` → `{ref, indicator, refresh, refreshing}`: `ref` on the SCROLLER (gets `overscroll-behavior-y: contain`), `indicator` as its first child (zero-height sticky + an always-mounted `aria-live="polite"` region — deliberately no `role="status"`, specs elsewhere look that role up strictly). Touch only, passive; starts only at `scrollTop 0` (nested scrollers too), never with a text field focused / started on one, inside a sideways scroller, with two fingers or for a sideways drag; arms at 64 px of pull (finger travel halved); reduced motion → `data-still`, nothing follows the finger. It calls the list's EXISTING refetch; a TanStack result with `isError` or a rejection reads "Couldn’t refresh". Mounted on: Inbox (`list.refetch`), Messages (the four list queries), Trash (`trash.refetch`), the sidebar / Browse drawer (`refetchQueries(["vault"], active)`); each has a **Refresh button** calling `refresh()` (the tree's is the existing "Refresh vault"). The Trash no longer focuses its search field on touch devices (the keyboard hid the list and blocked the pull). Row swipes (`useSwipeActions`, unchanged): Trash rows → Restore (either way; delete is never a swipe); Messages unread EMAIL rows → right = Mark as read, only when `useLiveActions("email")` gives a client, with the same action as a small button on the row (`useEmailRowActions`; the lists are updated in place, then re-read). **Archive is never a swipe** (it moves mail in the real mailbox, no undo) — it stays a button in the opened email; chat rows have no such action. The Trash scroller wraps a `role="list"` that holds list items only (the pull indicator sits outside it). Specs: `notion-pull.spec.ts` (fixtures `notion-inbox`, `notion-gestures` (new: the Messages dashboard over fakes), `pages-nav`).

## Native links and ⌘N (w11-native; `docs/client-app.md` § Links and New Page)

- **One allowlist in three places — keep them in step:** server `apps/server/src/routes/app-links.ts` (`APP_LINK_ROUTES`/`APP_LINK_EXCLUDED`), shell `apps/client/src-tauri/src/links.rs`, page `apps/web/src/native/appLinks.ts`. Routes that open in the app: `/page/<id>`, `/collab/<id>` (→ the page), `/inbox[/<id>]`, `/agent[/<uuid>]`. 🔒 NEVER add `/auth/*`, `/accept-invite`, `/p/*` or anything with a query: the device sign-in opens `/auth/device/authorize` in the system browser and returns via loopback / `prism://auth/callback` — an app that captured those breaks PKCE; a capability link (`/collab/<id>?t=`) would lose its access in the app (the association file excludes it by query, `links.rs` refuses every query).
- **Server:** `GET /.well-known/apple-app-site-association` + `/apple-app-site-association` (mounted above the SPA fallback; public, `application/json`, no redirect, `max-age=3600`; `applinks` only). `APPLE_APP_ID` (`<TeamID>.<bundle id>`, comma list; unset = `83Y42N33H8.com.benjaminlife.prism.client`; `""` → 404). **Served only when the request host NAME equals `APP_ORIGIN`'s** (`isAppHost`; an alias / tunnel name / IP → 404 — after a deploy `curl` the public host: a proxy that rewrites `Host` would 404 it). Exclusions first: the server-owned paths, then `{"/":"*","?":"?*"}` (**any URL with a query stays in the browser** — the app refuses every query, so claiming one would dead-end) and `/<route>/*/*`. Both paths are in the SW `navigateFallbackDenylist` (`check:sw` evaluates them). Test `test/app-links.test.ts`.
- **Shell:** tao delivers custom-scheme AND universal links as `RunEvent::Opened` (macOS + iOS) — no deep-link plugin, no IPC command for links. `links::parse(raw, origin)`: origin must EQUAL the configured server (`ServerOrigin` form), no userinfo/query, ids `[A-Za-z0-9_-]`, raw backslash/control/space refused. **Validate first** (`classify`/`accept`, pure + unit-tested): only a valid link foregrounds the window; a refused https link → a toast; an unknown `prism://…` (any web page can fire one) and the sign-in redirect (`prism://auth/…`, any case, also `prism:auth/…`) → nothing visible; nothing of a refused URL is stored or logged. The page gets a canonical path REBUILT from validated parts through `__PRISM_SHELL__.openLink(path)` → payload-free `prism:open-link` → `takePendingLink()` → `openTab` (never a navigation; access = the account's). One pending link (`LinkState`), handed over once the page is loaded and a device token EXISTS, dropped after 10 min. **The shell cannot tell a stale token from a good one**, so the page takes links before the sign-in gate (`captureAppLinks`) and keeps the validated PATH in `sessionStorage` (`prism:pending-link`, ≤ 10 min) across the sign-in reload; `initAppLinks` opens it in the signed-in workspace. host.js itself may not use web storage (`verify-client.mjs`).
- **Entitlement is generated per install** (`apps/client/scripts/universal-links.mjs`; `gen/universal-links/` is git-ignored; `verify-client.mjs` fails if a host/entitlement is committed). macOS: `associated-domains` is a RESTRICTED entitlement — an ad-hoc build carrying it is killed at launch; it needs team signing + an embedded provisioning profile (`--profile`). `src-tauri/Info.plist` registers only the `prism` scheme.
- **⌘N:** File → New Page (`CmdOrCtrl+N`, `menu.rs`) → `prism:new-page` → `useKeyboardShortcuts` → `usePagesUI.openCreate({})`; never behind a modal; menu + keydown for one press = one page. On Apple platforms the KEY binding is ⌘N only (Ctrl+N is "next line"). `lib/shortcuts.ts` `NATIVE_ONLY_SHORTCUTS`/`shortcutAvailable`: the sheet row and the ⌘K hint (`shortcutKeys` → `undefined`) exist only in a native shell.
- **Unverified without a signed build + device:** the OS hand-off of universal links, the menu accelerator, iOS hardware-keyboard ⌘N, the export save panel. Spec: `native-shell.spec.ts` runs the shell's REAL `host.js` in a browser against the real-server fixture.
- **Export archives in the client: `save_export {jobId, suggestedName, cancel?}`** (`src-tauri/src/export_archive.rs`; docs/client-app.md "Saving an export archive"). The webview CANCELS `<a download>` (no download handler — never add one: `verify-client.mjs` fails on it), so the shell saves: the page passes a job id (strict 22-char shape) and a name — 🔒 never a URL, path or token; Rust builds `<configured origin>/api/export/<id>/download`, sends the keychain bearer there only, **follows no redirect**, requires 200 + `application/zip`, caps declared and streamed bytes, and streams to a fresh hidden `.part` beside the path the NATIVE save panel returned, renamed only when complete (removed on failure/stop; an existing file is untouched). Progress = `prism:export-save-progress`; `cancel: true` stops that job's save; one save at a time. Page: `nativeExportSaver()` (`lib/import-export/client.ts`) — `ExportDialog` uses it only when `__PRISM_SHELL__.saveExport` exists; the browser path is unchanged. iOS: the same command ends in the system share sheet (see *iOS app*). The IPC lists are pinned per platform in `verify-client.mjs` §9 (desktop: 9 main + `quick_capture`; iOS: 14). Tests: `cargo test --lib export_archive::`, `native-export.spec.ts`. Unverified without a built app: the save panel and a real multi-GB stream.

## iOS app (WP5, `feat/w13-ios-merge`; `docs/client-app.md` § iOS app = pieces table + the owner's build/device procedure)

**State: merged as code. Host tests + `verify-client.mjs` + fixture specs pass; the `cfg(target_os = "ios")` Rust and the Swift plugin (`apps/client/src-tauri/plugins/prism-ios`) have NEVER been compiled for iOS or run on a device.** Never `tauri build` / `tauri ios …` / `xcodebuild` / simulators / signing on the production host; cargo there = host `cargo check` / `cargo test --lib` only.
- **macOS is unchanged.** Everything iOS is `#[cfg(target_os = "ios")]` / `#[cfg(mobile)]`; `capabilities/default.json` + `quick-capture.json` are desktop-only (`platforms`), `capabilities/mobile.json` is iOS-only with its OWN pinned list of 14 commands: the six shared ones, `export_note` + `save_export` (both end in the share sheet), and `reset_server`, `get_app_settings`, `set_app_lock`, `push_register`, `push_status`, `push_take_opened` (`mobile_cmds.rs`; an error on desktop). The Swift plugin registers NO webview command — only Rust calls it (`ios.rs`), and it evaluates exactly one fixed script (`prism:native-push-opened`).
- **Optional server origin.** `AppState.origin()` is `Option` (`require_origin()` for server-bound commands). Desktop always has one (saved or built-in); iOS starts with none: `build_csp_for(None)` reaches NO remote origin, `ServerSetupScreen` (the page pre-checks obvious mistakes and contacts nothing) → `set_server_origin` (mobile arm: only while none is set) → `ServerOrigin::parse` → `auth::probe_server` (`GET /health?live=1`, no credential, no redirect, ≤ 4 KB) → saved → applied IN PLACE (`window.rs` `retarget_page`: CSP re-pointed + `<meta name="prism-server-origin">`; host.js reads the origin from that meta on iOS; `get_token {origin}` answers only for the current server). `links::accept(…, None, …)` refuses every link and keeps nothing; a server change calls `LinkState::clear()`.
- **🔒 App lock × content.** The page keeps running behind the lock cover (a native window above everything), so nothing may be HANDED to it while locked: `links::deliver` waits for `LinkState.lock_ready` (set in `lib.rs` after the saved lock was applied at launch) and for Swift `waitUnlocked` (`LockPolicy.mayDeliverLink`: no lock, or unlocked + active + no background trip awaiting its lock decision — a link that foregrounds the app arrives BEFORE `didBecomeActive` decides); `take_deliverable(signed_in, unlocked, now)`; the 10-minute TTL still applies. A tapped notification is held the same way (`takeOpenedSession` answers nothing while locked; the page is pinged after the unlock). New native → page hand-offs must take the same gate.
- **Sign-in on iOS never goes through `links.rs`.** `ASWebAuthenticationSession` returns `prism://auth/callback?…` to Swift → `signin.rs` → `pkce::code_from_redirect` (exact redirect + state) → `exchange_code` with the verifier. The app also registers the `prism` scheme (`Info.ios.plist`, for `prism://page/…`); `links.rs` still drops everything under `prism://auth` silently.
- **Push.** `apps/web/src/native/apnsPush.ts` = the `PushClient` on iOS for EVERY signed-in account (the PWA's web push stays owner-only in `main.tsx`): register on the toggle and on every launch while on (`POST /api/push/apns {token, environment}` via `serverFetch`), `DELETE` when turned off; the choice is per account (`prism:apns:<tag>`); `push_take_opened` answers a canonical PATH (`links::notification_path` → `/agent/<uuid>` | `/inbox/<id>`) opened through `appLinks.openAppLink`. Environment rule: `mobile_cmds::apns_environment` (no embedded profile = production).
- **Export on iOS.** `export_note` / `save_export` write under `<app tmp>/prism-exports/<random>/` (0700; `export_archive::share_dir`), Swift `shareFile` presents `UIActivityViewController` for a `.zip`/`.md`/`.html` under that folder only, Rust deletes the folder afterwards and purges leftovers at launch.
- **Per-build, never committed:** `universal-links.mjs --ios` patches the two `gen/apple/prism-client_iOS/*.entitlements` with the server host — restore them after the build (`verify-client.mjs` fails while a host is there). `ios-release.sh -adhoc` = the device-pass build (ad hoc profile, production APNs, nothing uploaded); without the flag = the App Store Connect export — **TestFlight only after full parity sign-off (owner rule)**.
- **Tests.** `cargo test --lib` (links: no-server + lock gate + notification paths; auth: probe; export_archive: share folder), `bash apps/client/scripts/ios-policy-tests/run.sh` (Swift lock rules on the Mac), `ios-shell.spec.ts` (fixture `ios-shell.html`: the REAL `host.js` with platform `ios` over a scripted IPC — setup screen, APNs calls, lock setting, taps). Fixture specs that inject `host.js` must replace BOTH placeholders (`__PRISM_ORIGIN__`, `__PRISM_PLATFORM__`).

## Document Editor

**Editor depth (wave 4A, schema v5).** Shared by the plain and the live editor; everything below is view-only except the four v5 schema changes (`editor/blocks.ts`, server tests `test/editor-blocks-v5.test.ts`, REST gate markers `child-page` / `data-heading-level` / `data-col-width` / `data-cell-color`).
- **`lib/tiptap/EditorKeys.ts`** (priority 1100, in both editors): **block selection** — Esc selects the caret's block (or every block a text range touches), ↑/↓ move it, Shift+↑/↓ extend, Enter returns to the text, Backspace/Delete remove, ⌘⇧↑/↓ move (also Alt+⇧), ⌘D duplicates (a list item when only a caret; `freshCopy` gives mention chips new uids and no reminder), ⌘↵ checks a to-do / flips a toggle. Esc is taken only when none of the EDITOR'S OWN visible popups is open (`EDITOR_POPUPS`), and **inside a row peek (`.db-peek`) Esc closes the peek**: ProseMirror preventDefaults every editor Escape and `RowPeek` ignores used keys, so the editor blurs and re-dispatches an unused Escape. One block = a real `NodeSelection`; several = a text range + plugin state `blockSelectionKey` drawn as `.prism-block-selected`; any other selection/doc change clears it; both bubble menus skip it (`blockSelectionActive`). **⌘K with a non-empty text selection opens the inline link field** (`EDIT_LINK_EVENT` → `SelectionActions`); with no selection the key is not handled, so the shell's quick find still opens (`useKeyboardShortcuts` skips `defaultPrevented`). **⌘⇧H** = highlight in the last colour picked (`rememberHighlightColor`, localStorage). **⌘Z right after a Markdown conversion restores the typed characters**: TipTap's own `undoInputRule` state is cleared by the trailing-node rule's appended transaction, so a tracker plugin keeps the rule transform + appended transactions and inverts all of it. `>>` + space = toggle (Typography's « » are off in BOTH editors' configs — keep them in step).
- **Clipboard (`lib/tiptap/markdownClipboard.ts`).** Copy puts Markdown in `text/plain` (a selection inside one text block stays plain text; `[[wikilinks]]` are never escaped); plain text with no `text/html` pastes as blocks when `looksLikeMarkdown` says so — ONE strong signal (fence, `##`+ heading, task item, table) or TWO weak ones, and not program source; decided by `markdownSignals`, a per-line LINEAR scanner (**no regex over pasted text**: the first version's multi-line regexes took 54 s on 60k blank lines; `test/markdown-clipboard.test.ts` is time-bounded), ≤ `MARKDOWN_PASTE_MAX` (50 KB, larger → plain text + a notice) and ≤ 400 emphasis delimiters per paragraph (what makes `marked` quadratic); never with the caret in code (block or inline) nor after ⌘⇧V; wikilinks are lifted out before `marked`. **TipTap's per-mark paste rules are OFF in both editors (`enablePasteRules: false`)** — this is the only Markdown paste rule; a list whose EVERY item starts with a checkbox (input, or Notion's `div.checkbox`) pastes as a to-do list. **Stored Markdown follows the same rule (slice N):** `lib/html/taskLists.ts` `taskListsInHtml` (pure, no DOM, one linear pass over the Markdown parser's HTML; `@prism/core/task-lists`) turns a list whose every item is a GFM task item into `ul[data-type=taskList] > li[data-type=taskItem][data-checked]` (nested lists judged on their own items; `ol` too) and, in a MIXED list, writes each box back as the text `[x] ` / `[ ] `. It tokenises like an HTML parser (tags of any name with their quoted attribute values, comments / CDATA, raw-text elements; an unterminated construct ends the scan) so nothing inside an attribute, a comment or a `<textarea>` is read or rewritten. **Writers:** `addTaskListRule(turndown)` (same module) is the ONE list-item rule — a to-do item is written `- [x] ` / `- [ ] `, a plain item's leading literal `[x] ` / `[ ] ` is not escaped (a mixed list is stable across saves; a plain list whose EVERY item starts with such a marker therefore reads back as a to-do list) — installed in `convert/core.ts` `plainTurndown` (MCP note resource) + `blocksTurndown` (Move to) and the web shell's `html_to_markdown`; the `.md` export's own writer (`feat/w9-gaps` `transfer/export-markdown.ts`) can adopt it at integration. Called by the server's `markdownToHtmlSync` (`convert/core.ts`: worker and inline lane; the pre-check is unchanged — same input, linear extra work), `convertApi.markdownToHtml` (every shell's plain editor) and `markdownToPasteHtml`. No schema change. Tests: `test/markdown-tasks.test.ts` (time-bounded), `markdown-todos.spec.ts`. Not covered: the HTML export of a Markdown-bodied page (`transfer/worker.ts` `to-html` calls `marked` itself). `view.pasteHTML` re-enters `handlePaste` — keep the re-entrancy guard. A URL pasted over a selection links it.
- **Placeholders:** `editorPlaceholder(emptyDocText)` for the Placeholder extension (per block: "Type '/' for commands", "Heading N", "List", "To-do", "Toggle", "Quote"); CSS shows it only while focused (an empty document always), never read-only.
- **Block menu (`BlockHandles`)**: searchable `EditorMenu` (`searchable` + `searchItems`; focus starts on the first item, typing moves into the field), Copy (HTML + Markdown), **Move to** another page (`lib/tiptap/moveBlock.ts` → `POST /api/notes/:id/blocks/append` via `serverFetch`, never the VaultClient/outbox: the block is removed here only on a confirmed `200 {ok:true}` and only if unchanged; then `copyAttachments(target)` gives the target its own files, or the notice states the limitation; hidden on the legacy desktop), Comment (live docs, `onComment`), **Ask agent** (selects the block's text, then the same `useSelectionAsk().ask("selection")`). A handle dragged from inside a block selection moves every selected block (`moveTopBlockIn(…, count)`: one step plain, delete+insert live). **Columns from a drop:** the right ~12 % of the column or the left margin beyond the handles (`x < left − 60`) = "beside" (`moveBlocksBesideIn`: wraps target + dragged in `columns`, or adds a column up to 5; ONE transaction in the plain editor, **delete then replace-target in a live document** — one transaction makes y-prosemirror rewrite every block in between and misplaces collaborators' typing, `test/editor-beside-live.test.ts`). `columnsView.ts` = gutter handles (role separator; drag commits ONE transaction on release, ←/→ nudge, double-click equalises).
- **Node-view gotcha (fixed):** a node view must not read `editor.view` while the INITIAL document's views are built (TipTap throws) — the toggle view did, so a stored page containing a toggle failed to open; use `document` / `globalThis.document`.
- **Sub-pages (NP-PG-15).** `childPage` stores the page id ONLY (`<div data-type="child-page" data-page-id>`); `lib/tiptap/childPage.tsx` renders the row through the reader's own `getNote` (unviewable → "No access", like the page mention) and `ChildPages.configure({create, hostPath, trash})` is the host seam: slash **Page** → `createSubPage` (`lib/tiptap/subPages.ts`, `<parent path>/<unique name>`, the gateway decides), a `prism:page-created {id, parentPath}` window event (fired by `lib/pages/quickCreate.ts`) adds a row to the OPEN parent, and deleting a row offers "Move to Trash" ONLY when `describe(id)` (the deleter's own read) shows the page directly under this page's path — then it is named; never after a cut (`uiEvent: cut`), a "Move to" (`suppressTrashOffer`) or a collaborator's change; decided 250 ms later so a move is not a delete. `createSubPage` refuses while offline and refuses a returned `offline-…` id (`SubPageError`): a document never stores a temporary id. "Create page" from `[[` maps the typed range through every transaction during the async create and replaces it only if `[[query` is still there. A parent that is not open gets no row. `[[` → "Create page '<query>'" uses the same helper (`WikilinkDropdown` `hostPath`).
- **Shortcut sheet:** `components/renderers/ShortcutSheet.tsx` self-mounts on `<body>`; **⌘/ opens it everywhere, the editor included; the block menu is ⌘⇧/**; command bar "Keyboard Shortcuts". App-level keys come from ONE table, `lib/shortcuts.ts` (`APP_SHORTCUTS`: sidebar ⌘\\, side panel ⌘⇧\\ — the shell branch's bindings; change a binding there and in `useKeyboardShortcuts`). No key is listed with two meanings (⌘K is the one contextual key: link with a selection, quick find without).
- **`POST /api/notes/:id/blocks/append {html, requestId}`** (`routes/blocks.ts`, mounted before the owner short-circuit): signed-in accounts, CSRF, 30/min, `edit` on the target (unviewable/trashed → 404, system → 403, locked → 409, non-document → 400), strict id. The html is parsed through the shared schema (only valid content is stored). Target LIVE → appended through the live Y.Doc (`appendToLiveDoc`: current doc + blocks → `updateYFragment`, so only new elements are created and unsaved typing survives — a PATCH would be folded over it by the reconciler); else one CAS write (retried once), Markdown bodies get Markdown (`blocksHtmlToMarkdown` in the conversion worker; Prism-only blocks stay HTML). Every conversion in this route goes through `convert/service.ts` (`canonicalBlocks`/`appendToBody` are async; unconvertible blocks → 413 `too_complex`, a busy converter → 503 `busy`), and the live page's size comes from `ensureRenderedSize`/`renderedSizeOf`. Idempotent per (vault, note, account, `requestId`) via `block_append_receipts` (`block-append-store.ts`; same id + other body → 422). The live path writes the receipt IN THE SAME TICK as the append (`live = 2`, `applied = "<yjs client>:<clock>"`): a request answered 503 `not_confirmed` after the blocks entered the document is retried without a second copy — the retry finds them by that marker (loaded document, else snapshot), stores once more, and answers 200 only when durable; a marker no state covers (document lost before it was saved) voids the receipt.
- **Suggestion marks vs strike/underline:** `insertion`/`deletion` rank before the formatting marks (priority 1200 → their `<span>` is OUTERMOST) and carry a low-priority `text-decoration-color` style rule with `clearMark`, so re-seeding stored HTML no longer adds `<s>`/`<u>` under a suggestion (`test/suggestion-roundtrip.test.ts`). Consequence: a suggestion over several formatted runs is ONE span (documents holding suggestions re-serialise once). (The server's Markdown/HTML conversions run in the conversion worker — see *Content conversion — NEVER on the main thread*.)
- **REST gate markers** (`needsEditorUpdate`, api.ts) are a linear scan that reads attributes like an HTML parser (any case, either quote or none, whitespace around `=`).
- **Find contract for shells:** `window.dispatchEvent(new CustomEvent("prism:find-in-page"))` (= `requestFindInPage()`) opens the find bar of the focused editor, else the one on screen (a peek wins over the page under it).

`DocumentRenderer` uses TipTap 3 (ProseMirror). Wikilinks (`[[target]]`) are rendered via `WikilinkDecoration` (a ProseMirror plugin in `src/lib/tiptap/WikilinkMark.ts`) — it uses `font-size: 0` + CSS `::after` to show only the clean display name. `WikilinkAutocomplete` (`src/lib/tiptap/WikilinkAutocomplete.ts`) provides `[[` completion against vault paths. Auto-save is handled by `useAutoSave` hook (debounced, calls `onSave`).

## Sync Adapters

`src-tauri/src/sync/adapters/` contains adapters for: `google_docs`, `notion`, `notion_db`, `github`. Each adapter implements the sync engine's trait. GitHub sync pushes vault notes as markdown files to a repo. Notion DB sync bidirectionally maps Parachute notes to Notion database rows using `PropertyMapping` config stored in `NotionDbSyncConfig` — its pull honors `conflict_strategy` via `should_overwrite()` (notion-wins / parachute-wins / newer-wins) and skips no-op writes.

**Server ports (Client parity B, `docs/sync.md`).** GitHub FOLDER sync and Notion DATABASE sync run on the Prism Server with stored configs (SQLite `github_sync_configs` / `notion_db_sync_configs`, `worker/sync-store.ts`) and the vault's stored `github` / `notion` secret; routes `/api/sync/github/*` and `/api/sync/notion-db/*` (vault-scoped, never return a token). **SERVER-OWNER only** (review H2): an allow-list middleware in `routes/sync.ts` leaves vault admins only `GET /github/configs`, `GET /notion-db/configs`, `GET /audit`; owner writes need `Content-Type: application/json` (415 otherwise); `github`/`notion` credential PUT/DELETE are owner-only like matrix/google. `POST /api/sync/github/import` maps the desktop's `github-sync-configs.json` with auto-sync forced OFF. Exports are filtered to what the config's `created_by` can view (`worker/sync-visibility.ts`; never another user's private note); auto-sync into a PUBLIC repo needs `allowPublic`; Notion→vault mappings and tags can't use reserved names (`worker/sync-reserved.ts`: `prism_*`, `gov_*`, skill keys, `agent-skill`, `governance-*`, …); no `contentProperty` = the body is never synced. Both GitHub clients (incl. the stateless `/github/push|pull`, review H1) validate owner/repo/branch, encode every URL segment and use `redirect: "error"`; `safeRepoPath` also refuses every dot-segment and git verify_path `.git` variants; only the sync extension is ever written. GitHub uses the **Git Data API, no clone** (`worker/github-dir.ts`): one commit per push, local git-blob-SHA comparison (unchanged files cost nothing), `blob_map` for real conflict detection (local-wins overwrites / remote-wins keeps), `safeRepoPath` (no `..`, absolute, `.git`, control chars), size caps, fast-forward-only ref update. **Auto-sync** (`worker/github-folder.ts`) listens to `subscribeTreeChanges` (no polling), debounces dirty notes under the folder into ONE commit (`GITHUB_AUTOSYNC_DEBOUNCE_MS` 30 s / `_MAX_WAIT_MS` 5 min), a `resync` → full push; `manual` strategy never auto-pushes; `GITHUB_AUTOSYNC_ENABLED=false` kills it. Notion DB (`worker/notion-db.ts`) keeps `should_overwrite`/no-op skip, adds `if_updated_at` (409 = conflict), a push that skips pages already matching the note, 3 rps/token limiter + 429 retry; background pass only with `NOTION_DB_SYNC_ENABLED=true` (`NOTION_DB_SYNC_INTERVAL_MS` 10 min). Every push/sync runs under a per-config lock and writes a `sync_audit` row (`GET /api/sync/audit`). Client: `HostServices.githubSync` / `notionDbSync` via `useGitHubSyncApi` / `useNotionDbSyncApi` (owner); "Notion Database Sync…" is a command-bar entry (`NotionDbSyncHost` in `Shell`). Tests inject fakes (`setGitHubFetchForTests`, `setGitHubVaultForTests`, `setGitHubFeedForTests`, `setNotionDbFetchForTests`, `setNotionDbVaultForTests`); never let one reach GitHub/Notion.

**Matrix message ingest** is server-side too (`apps/server/src/worker/matrix.ts`, 60s; desktop `message_sync` is disabled by `disable_message_sync`). One `message-thread` note per room, **updated in place** — so `createdAt` says nothing about freshness. **Rollover (2026-09-30, `worker/matrix-rollover.ts`):** vault 0.7.9 refuses to update any note over 2 MB while history is on (413 `history_overflow`), so once a thread passes `MATRIX_THREAD_MAX_BYTES` (1 MB) its oldest messages move into immutable `<threadPath>/archive/NNN` notes (tag `message-archive`, never `message-thread`, no `matrixRoomId` — the room→note map skips them) and the live note keeps its header, an `> Earlier messages: [[…]]` pointer and the newest ~200 KB. Archives are written before the trim, and a crash between is detected and completed (never duplicated or dropped); every ingest/reconcile pass sweeps oversized threads. **🔒 Lean listing (2026-10-03):** both passes list `message-thread` rows with NO content and only `THREAD_LIST_KEYS` (`matrixRoomId`, `lastMessageAt`, `archiveOf`); a thread's body is read in exactly one place, `loadThread` (one `getNote` by id), immediately before a write to that note — the append (dedupe, metadata merge, triage-tag strip and append-time rollover all use that fresh copy) and the sweep's rollover of a thread whose lean `byteSize` (vault 0.7.x `NoteIndex`, UTF-8 bytes) exceeds the limit (re-checked on the fresh body). **Never build a write from a listing row.** A vault whose rows carry no `byteSize` is sized by ≤ `SIZE_PROBES_PER_PASS` (10) body reads per pass, once per `updatedAt`; the append path measures the fresh body regardless. The reconcile probe (`messagesBefore(…, {cap:1})`) asks the homeserver for `limit=1` (later pages 100; a small first page with no usable message keeps paging). Pinned by `test/matrix-lean.test.ts` — a GOLDEN run of scripted syncs through the pre-change ingester (`test/fixtures/matrix-ingest-3c1df0a.ts`, verbatim) and the lean one must leave identical vaults. Note the vault still READS bodies for a lean list (`toNoteIndex` computes `byteSize`/`preview` from content); what is saved is serialising, sending and parsing them. **One INGEST pass per vault at a time (review B1):** `runMatrixOnce` JOINS a running pass (never a second one from the same cursor — a fresh body read would append the same events again), the tick skips while one runs (no outcome → a hung pass reads `stale`), `POST /api/integrations/matrix/sync` answers 409 `busy`. The guard covers ingest + cursor persistence ONLY: the hourly reconcile and the bridge resync are started afterwards in the background under their OWN per-vault guard (`matrixReconcileRunning`), so ingest keeps its 60 s cadence during a sweep; `reconcileMatrix` stops probing/repairing at `MATRIX_RECONCILE_DEADLINE_MS` (20 min; `unprobed` rooms wait for the next sweep, retried in 5 min). Ingest ∥ reconcile on one thread is safe by the next rule. **The append is compare-and-set** (`ifUpdatedAt` = the copy just read; rollover with `noRetry`): a 409 (by the error's STATUS, never its text) → re-read → drop what is already there → recount → ONE retry; a second 409 fails the room. **Dedupe identity (replay, reconcile, non-exact gap-fill, the 409 path) = stamp + body, NOT the rendered sender** (`messageKey`/`entryKey`; trailing whitespace ignored; each stored copy cancels one incoming copy): the same event is "251731455: hi" when a member lookup failed and "Rin: hi" a pass later. **A failed room is replayed (S2):** `ingestMatrix` returns `failedRooms` (write / body read / gap-fill failed while the cursor advances); the scheduler persists them with THAT pass's cursor (worker cursor `matrix-replay`, written BEFORE the main cursor) and the next pass first re-fetches `messagesBefore({from: <current cursor>, to: <failed pass's cursor>})` — or, for a room with NO thread note yet, pages to the room's START like the new-room gap-fill — and appends with dedupe; a member lookup that fails inside a replay fails the replay. `MATRIX_REPLAY_MAX_TRIES` (10) failed replays → the room is given up on: recorded under cursor `matrix-lost`, and **while that list is non-empty the `matrix` source reads `failing` in `/acl/workers`** (count + room ids; one alert per episode through the ordinary health path) and those rooms are NOT queued for replay again. The owner looks with `GET /acl/workers/matrix/lost` and clears with `DELETE /acl/workers/matrix/lost` (server owner, `Content-Type: application/json` + CSRF checks, audited `admin.matrix-lost-clear`). **Bounded calls (S1):** vault calls of a pass time out after `MATRIX_VAULT_TIMEOUT_MS` (30 s); every homeserver request of the ingest client — reads, join, leave, the management-room send — after `MATRIX_READ_TIMEOUT_MS` (120 s). Tests: `test/matrix-overlap.test.ts`. Not guarded the same way: `runClickUpOnce` / `runFathomOnce` / `runFirefliesOnce` have no in-flight guard and the tick itself has none (Proton has `running`, calendar `withLock`, Gmail claims its slot up front). **Residuals (accepted):** on the dedupe paths two different messages with the same minute + same text count as one identity (a copy already in the note cancels one incoming copy — a genuinely repeated message can be dropped there); a sender name containing `": "` is split at the first one, so such an entry may not be recognised (→ a duplicate, on those paths only); a multi-line body whose continuation line itself looks like `[YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM] ` is parsed as a new entry; after an outcome-unknown ROLLOVER write, an appended batch larger than `MATRIX_THREAD_KEEP_BYTES` has partly moved into an archive, where the replay's dedupe does not look (→ those lines again); a restart between a pass's writes and its cursor write re-syncs the same range WITHOUT dedupe (the incremental path never dedupes). Use use `updatedAt`/`metadata.lastMessageAt`. Lines are `[YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM] Display Name: body` (UTC, desktop format); `messageCount`/`participants` are maintained (participants unioned, since incremental `/sync` carries member deltas only). On append the ingester strips `triaged` + the four importance tags so the hourly `message-triage` skill re-classifies the thread. **Invites auto-join** (`MATRIX_AUTO_JOIN=true`, `MATRIX_AUTO_JOIN_PER_RUN` throttled to Synapse's rc_joins; un-joinable invites (404/403) are formally rejected so they can't wedge the queue) — mautrix invites the sync user to every new chat portal, and an un-joined room is invisible to `/sync`, so this must stay on or new chats are silently dropped. **Capacity gotcha:** bulk room-joins fan out into the local-model classify skill — pace them (PER_RUN small) or the resident 12B model swap-storms the 16GB host (see 2026-08-24 incident in the vault report `vault/agent/reports/2026-08-23-prism-matrix-ingest-audit`). **No-drop layers (2026-09-23):** (1) a `limited` sync timeline is gap-filled from `prev_batch` back to the previous cursor (new rooms page to their start) — `/sync` only returns 30 events/room; (2) `reconcileMatrix` sweeps EVERY joined room hourly (`MATRIX_RECONCILE_MS`), comparing its newest message to the note's `lastMessageAt` (monotonic) and fetching gaps; (3) `MATRIX_BRIDGE_RESYNC` sends `sync-chats` to the Telegram bridge management room every 3h. **Bridge-side gotcha:** mautrix-telegram's client dies on a missed ping ("engine was closed") while the container stays Up — `~/dev/omniharmonic_agent/matrix/bridge-watchdog.sh` (pm2 `bridge-watchdog`) restarts it; bridge `backfill.enabled` must stay true or every such gap is lost for good. Telegram forum topics only get portals when a message arrives; to force one, `create-portal channel:<id>:<topicId>` — which says "No portal found" unless a `portal` row already exists (seed one by copying an mxid-less row in the bridge DB; topic ids are in gitcoin-brain `telegram/*` notes' `metadata.topic_id`). The bridge cannot backfill history into an EXISTING room on vanilla Synapse, so a portal created before backfill was on stays truncated until it is recreated (`doin <room> delete-portal` → re-seed row → `create-portal`), then the note's `matrixRoomId` is re-pointed at the new room and the sync cursor advanced past the backfill (else the exact gap-fill re-appends it). Sender names come from `resolveDisplayNames` (joined members → `/profile`) on every pass — incremental `/sync` carries no member state, which is how 5.9k lines ended up as bare bridge ids before 2026-09-27.

**Source health + staleness alerts (WP0.5).** `apps/server/src/worker/health.ts` is the registry behind `GET /acl/workers` (server-owner only; 403 for admins/anon). `noteIngestOutcome` (scheduler) is still the single writer of ingest outcomes and forwards to `recordSourceOutcome` (last success / scrubbed last error / streak); status = `disabled` (no credential, or no note of that kind ever) | `failing` (streak >= `WORKER_FAIL_STREAK`, 3) | `stale` (nothing succeeded within the threshold; never-succeeded is measured from server boot) | `ok`. **Desktop-owned sources (email, calendar, skills) are INFERRED** from the newest vault note (email: tag `email` under `vault/messages/email/`; calendar: tag `meeting` under `vault/meetings/` with `metadata.calendarEventId`; skills: tag `agent-dispatch`), probed with `limit` + `order_by=updated_at` at most every `WORKER_DESKTOP_PROBE_MS` (3 min) and re-maxed client-side — so a genuinely quiet inbox/calendar reads as stale; tune or zero the threshold. Thresholds (`WORKER_STALE_<NAME>_MS`, 0 = no staleness check, failures still count): matrix 15 min, clickup 30 min, fireflies 30 h, fathom 0, email 12 h, calendar 24 h, skills 6 h (desktop sources are inferred from note activity, so short thresholds false-alarm on quiet days); index = 3x `INDEX_INTERVAL_MS`. Each tick `runHealthCheckOnce` alerts ONCE per episode on entry to stale/failing (email to `OWNER_EMAIL` via `sendEmail`, console-only with no Resend key, plus a vault note tagged `alert` at `vault/agent/alerts/<date>-<hhmm>-<source>`) and once on recovery; the episode flag persists in the worker cursor table (`health-alerted:<source>`) so a restart does not re-alert. `WORKER_ALERTS_ENABLED=false` (set in `.env.test`) keeps status but sends nothing. Errors are scrubbed (bearer/key=value/long opaque strings) and capped at 200 chars. UI: ServerPanel "Ingest health" card via the optional `CollabSharing.getWorkerHealth` seam.

**ClickUp task sync** is server-side: `apps/server/src/worker/clickup.ts` polls `GET /v2/team/{id}/task` (assigned-to-me by default; credential kind `clickup` = `{apiKey, teamId?, spaceIds?, assignedOnly?}`) every `CLICKUP_INTERVAL_MS` (5 min), mirroring tasks to `vault/tasks/clickup/*` tagged `[task, clickup]`, deduped by `metadata.source_id`, cursor `cursor:clickup:<vaultId>` = max `date_updated` ms (clamped below failed writes). One-way pull; deletions + write-back are handled nightly by the vault-weave scheduled job via ClickUp MCP. **Parachute note**: vault ≤0.6 default-filled omitted schema'd enum fields with the FIRST enum value; 0.7+ fills only an explicit `default:` (declared in `tag-schemas.json` for workflow-state fields). Ingesters still write schema'd fields (e.g. `priority`) explicitly, and enum order still leads with the neutral value, so behaviour is identical on both.

**Gmail ingest (WP1.2)** is server-side behind `GMAIL_SYNC_ENABLED` (default **off** — never run it alongside the desktop's `email_sync`). `apps/server/src/worker/gmail.ts` is the port of `email_sync.rs` + the `gog` Gmail call: `gog gmail messages search <q> --max N --include-body --account <acct> --json` (identical argv; the injectable `GogRunner` is what tests fake — they never run the real CLI). Account = the existing `google` credential kind (`{account}`, `PUT /api/integrations/google`, the same one Google Docs sync uses). Cadence = the desktop's: one run per `GMAIL_INTERVAL_MS` slot (3 min), first pass per process `in:inbox newer_than:14d` max 100 (body backfill), then `newer_than:3h` max 30. **Note shape is the desktop's byte-for-byte** so the server converges on existing notes: one note per thread tagged `email`, path `vault/messages/email/<rust sanitize_path(subject)>-<last 6 chars of threadId>` (`rustSanitizePath` keeps Unicode alphanumerics like Rust's `is_alphanumeric`), same content layout (20 000-byte body cap, cut on a char boundary), same metadata keys (`type, platform, threadId, from, subject, date, labels, isUnread, messageCount, lastMessageAt` — epoch ms, naive gog dates read as UTC). Dedupe is by `metadata.threadId` over the **FULL** email set (lean list, no bodies — the desktop only looked at 500 notes), then by path. Deliberate changes: creates carry `if_exists: "update"` (never a 409); the `email-from` person link rides in the same write (`links`); a thread whose metadata, byte size and person link are unchanged is **not rewritten** (each PATCH on vault ≥0.7.9 is a history version — the desktop rewrote every thread every pass). Health: with the flag on, `email` is a SERVER source (`runGmailOnce` → `noteIngestOutcome(…, "email")`, configured = a `google` credential) and is no longer inferred from notes.
- **Cutover runbook (overseer).** `disable_email_sync=true` in the desktop `prism-config.json` → restart Prism.app → ensure the `google` credential is stored for the vault (`GET /api/integrations/google` → `configured: true`) and `gog` works for that account as the pm2 user → set `GMAIL_SYNC_ENABLED=true` in `apps/server/.env` → restart pm2 `prism-server` → watch **two** intervals (≈6 min; the first is the 14-day backfill): the log line `[worker] gmail primary: N msgs / T threads → +c created ~u updated =s unchanged` should show `created` only for genuinely new threads, and `GET /acl/workers` `email` = kind server/ok. Duplicate check (must print nothing): `curl -s -H "Authorization: Bearer $T" "$VAULT/api/notes?tag=email&limit=50000" | jq -r '.[].metadata.threadId // empty' | sort | uniq -d` (or in SQLite: `SELECT json_extract(metadata,'$.threadId') t, count(*) FROM notes WHERE path LIKE 'vault/messages/email/%' GROUP BY t HAVING count(*) > 1;`). Rollback: `GMAIL_SYNC_ENABLED=false` → restart pm2 → `disable_email_sync=false` → restart the desktop.
- **Gmail is NOT the email pipeline** (the account has no Gmail service). `assertConfig()` refuses to start with `GMAIL_SYNC_ENABLED=true` + `PROTON_SYNC_ENABLED=true` (two live writers of `vault/messages/email/`); a Proton SHADOW never affects Gmail.

**Proton Mail ingest (WP1.2b) — the real email source.** Email comes from Proton Mail Bridge (IMAP on `127.0.0.1:1143`). Until cutover it is written by the agent repo's `omniharmonic_agent/scripts/proton_mail.py sync`, which runs as the launchd user agent `com.omniharmonic.proton-mail` every 300 s. `apps/server/src/worker/proton.ts` is the server port, behind `PROTON_SYNC_ENABLED` (default **off**). `PROTON_SHADOW=true` connects, fetches and diffs, records intents, and makes **zero vault writes**; it wins over ENABLED. Full doc + runbook: `docs/runbook/proton-ingest.md`.
- **Note shape is the script's, byte-for-byte.** The parity tests check it against fixtures produced by the script's own functions (Python 3.9) on synthetic mail. `worker/proton-parse.ts` reproduces the Python `email` policy.default semantics the output depends on: header unfolding, RFC 2047, address re-rendering, `parsedate_to_datetime`, `html.unescape`, `str.strip`/`splitlines`, and Unicode `\b`/`\w` in the label regexes.
  - One note **per message**, tag `email`, path `vault/messages/email/<slugify(subject)[:80]|no-subject>-<sha256(messageId)[:8]>`.
  - Metadata keys, in order: `type, subject, from, to, date (host-local "YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM"), isUnread, labels, messageCount 1, threadId, messageId, lastMessageAt, source "proton-bridge", account, mailbox, uid`.
  - `labels` = folder, UNREAD, and header-derived BULK / AUTOMATED / SOCIAL / CHAT / PROMOTIONS / TRANSACTIONAL / DIRECT. The `message-classify` shortcuts read these.
  - `threadId` = the root of the References chain, skipping `@protonmail.internalid`.
- **Deviations** (all write or read less):
  - **ONE lean list per pass** (`tag=email`, `path_prefix`, `include_metadata=source,messageId,mailbox,uid,isUnread,labels`). Notes are matched by Message-ID. There are no per-note GETs, no get-by-path → create fallback, and no 409s (the script did about 500 GETs every 5 min).
  - A header-only window fetch. Full sources are fetched only for messages that have no note.
  - The flag refresh sends `PATCH {isUnread, labels}` with `if_updated_at`, re-reading and retrying once on a 409. It uses the script's label rule (UNREAD goes back at index 1), plus a stale `uid` fix.
  - Creates use `if_exists: "ignore"`. A path held by a note that isn't this message is never overwritten.
  - Existing content is never rewritten.
  - Person linking is off by default (`PROTON_LINK_PEOPLE` links only to EXISTING person notes; `PROTON_LINK_RECIPIENTS` adds `email-to` the same way — see the identity + linking layer).
  - No deletes, ever (the script never deleted either).
  - **Bounded work (security review).** Messages over `PROTON_MAX_MESSAGE_BYTES` (10 MB, by RFC822.SIZE from the ref fetch) are never downloaded: intent `skip-too-large`, no note. Each text part is cut at 500 KB before parsing. The HTML stripper and every whitespace strip are **linear scanners**: the regex forms were quadratic (ReDoS — 640 KB of `<` took minutes and blocked the event loop). **Never reintroduce an unanchored regex with an unbounded quantifier on mail text in `proton-parse.ts`**; `test/proton-ingest.test.ts` has time-bounded pathological inputs.
  - A message that fails to parse 3×, an oversize one, and a path collision are skip-listed per (mailbox, UIDVALIDITY) (cursor `proton-skips`), so they aren't re-downloaded every pass.
- **Window and limits** match the script: `UID SEARCH SINCE <UTC today−7d>` (`PROTON_SINCE_DAYS`); more than `PROTON_MAX_PER_PASS` (200) new → the highest UIDs; `PROTON_MAILBOXES=INBOX`. The mailbox is opened read-only, BODY.PEEK only.
- **Credential.** Kind `proton-bridge` = `{host, port, username, password, security: starttls|tls, certSha256}`, stored via `PUT /api/integrations/proton-bridge`, encrypted with `SECRETS_KEY`. All `/proton-bridge*` routes (GET/PUT/DELETE/`sync`) are **server-owner only**, not vault-admin. The password is never echoed. A PUT may omit it only for a no-change re-save; changing host/port/security/username/pin requires it again (else 400). A forced `sync` while a pass runs → 409 (never queued). WP1.5 adds optional `smtpPort` (1025) / `smtpSecurity` (starttls) / `smtpCertSha256` (= `certSha256`) for sending; changing them also requires the password.
  - The host must be loopback.
  - The cert pin is **required**, with no trust-on-first-use. It uses the same format as the script's `certFingerprint`.
  - The pin is checked in a subclassed imapflow `authenticate()`: after TLS, **before** LOGIN sends the password. `test/proton-ingest.test.ts` proves it on a real loopback TLS stub IMAP server (STARTTLS and direct TLS; needs `openssl` to mint a throwaway cert, else skipped): a wrong pin and a server without STARTTLS see zero LOGIN/AUTHENTICATE bytes, and the right pin reaches LOGIN.
  - A mailbox select that fails at the connection level fails the pass; only a server `NO` counts as "skipped".
  - `username` becomes `metadata.account`, so it must equal the script's login.
  - Errors and logs are scrubbed of the password.
- **Health:** `proton` is a SERVER source in shadow AND live (stale after `WORKER_STALE_PROTON_MS`, 1 h). When live, the inferred desktop `email` source is dropped.
- **Intents:** `GET /acl/workers/proton/intents[?action=create,update-flags&verify=1]` (server owner). Intents hold note ids, uids, the 8-hex path hash (`pathHash`), a Message-ID hash and content/metadata hashes — never a subject, slug, address or body. Failure reasons are reduced to `vault HTTP <status>`. `verify` finds the script's note by Message-ID hash (one lean list) and reports `match`, `differs` (with key names) or `missing`.
- **Cutover runbook (overseer only)**, in `docs/runbook/proton-ingest.md`:
  1. Back up.
  2. Shadow ≥24 h alongside the script, pulling `?verify=1` every few hours. Every `create` and `update-flags` intent should turn `match`. A `differs` on `date` alone means set `PROTON_TIMEZONE`.
  3. Retire the launchd job: `launchctl bootout gui/$(id -u)/com.omniharmonic.proton-mail && launchctl disable gui/$(id -u)/com.omniharmonic.proton-mail`. Keep `proton_mail.py`, its Keychain item and its state file: `sent_thread_ids()`, `health` and `search` still use them. The agent's `check_oauth.py` will alert on the script's stale `lastSync` after 6 h, so repoint it in the agent repo.
  4. Set `PROTON_SHADOW=false` and `PROTON_SYNC_ENABLED=true`, restart pm2, and watch two intervals.
  5. Duplicate check, which must print nothing: `…/api/notes?tag=email&limit=50000&include_metadata=source,messageId | jq -r '.[]|select(.metadata.source=="proton-bridge")|.metadata.messageId' | sort | uniq -d`.
- **Rollback:** set `PROTON_SYNC_ENABLED=false` and restart pm2, then `launchctl enable …` and `launchctl bootstrap gui/$(id -u) ~/Library/LaunchAgents/com.omniharmonic.proton-mail.plist`. Never run both writers live.

**Person linking (`apps/server/src/worker/people.ts`).** Shared port of `person_linker.rs`, used by Gmail (and Matrix behind a flag). The desktop's lookup (up to four FTS searches over 10–20 hits, plus a path check that compared `normalize("j--smith")` to `normalize("J. Smith")`) missed people who existed and then POSTed a duplicate path → the **create→409 storm** WP0.6 traced. `PeopleIndex.load` lists every `person` note ONCE per pass (lean, no cap) and indexes: email (lowercased) from `email`/`emails`/`contact`/`channels.email`; Matrix id from `matrix`/`matrixId`/`matrixRoomIds`/`channels.*`; normalized name from `metadata.name`, path leaf and title; and the exact `vault/people/<slug>` path. Lookup order email → Matrix id → name → path. A miss is created with `if_exists: "ignore"` (a path the listing couldn't see returns the existing note, never a 409) and added to the index, so one sender across many threads is created once. Skip rules are the desktop's (name < 3 bytes, >50% digits, bare address, noreply/role mailboxes). New person notes also get the schema's `email` field besides `channels.email`. **Since the identity layer (`src/identity.ts`, doc `docs/roadmap/workspace-experience/BACKEND-STATUS-GRAPH.md`) the index is PINNED against its 6069ccd behaviour by `test/people-parity.test.ts` (a verbatim copy of the old index in `test/fixtures/`, 846 cases): it never creates where the old one linked or skipped.** Keys are claimed as before; what changed: a tombstone claimant (PRECISELY: tag `merged-stub`/`superseded` or `status: merged_into_canonical` — a bare `merged_into`/`superseded_by` pointer on an untagged note leaves it a live person) redirects to its canonical person when the pointer is a note id or a full path (a bare leaf or a name is followed ONLY by the reviewed job, `refByName`); a claimant that is a non-human note (`bot`/`non-human`/`organization` tag or type) or an unresolvable tombstone still OWNS the key but is neither linked nor re-created (`ambiguous` + `claimed`); the layer's extra keys (addresses inside a multi-value string, `contact_emails`, puppet ids) are tried only when nobody claims the key the old way. A name match is `candidates`, never a link; with a `review` sink (`PEOPLE_QUEUE_ON_INGEST`) alias/slug-variant matches count too and the miss is queued instead of creating a duplicate.

**Identity + linking layer (owner-only `/api/admin/people/*`; every job dry-run, every flag off by default; job, merge and resolve mutually exclusive → 409 `busy`).** `IdentityIndex.match()` is conservative: exactly one live person on a strong key (email, Matrix id, Telegram id/@handle, phone WITH country code, bridge handle from a KNOWN mautrix prefix + id-shaped remote part) links; **a name never links by default** (review `name-only`) — `allowNameLinks` enables it for meeting attendees and task assignees only, never for email From names or chat display names; `metadata.title` is not a name; `GENERIC_NAMES` never match; a display name is never a strong key, however address-shaped; the owner by name = the note's own full name (`owner-full-name`) or an explicitly CONFIGURED alias (`owner-alias`; single tokens only for task assignees and never when shared). `src/relationships.ts` = canonical vocabulary (`messages-with`, `email-from`, `email-to`, `attended-by`, `has-transcript`, `assigned-to`, `belongs-to`, `member-of`, `works-at`, `references`, `related-to`); a synonym is rewritten only when both notes have one kind and the direction is unambiguous (only attended/attendee(s)/member/has-member may flip); `wikilink` is vault-managed. **Review queue:** SQLite `identity_candidates` (created by `src/identity-store.ts`, not `db.ts`; stores the key VALUE; logs/audit/samples carry hashes + ids) — `GET /candidates`, `POST /candidates/:id/resolve {personId, addIdentity?=false, applyToKey?}`, `…/dismiss`. **Owner:** `GET|PUT|DELETE /owner` stores the owner's person note (+ emails, first-name aliases) per vault in SQLite settings, falling back to `PEOPLE_OWNER_*`; never hardcoded. **Backfill job** `src/people-link-job.ts` (`POST /link {dryRun=true, phases?, maxWrites?, enqueue?, allowNameLinks?=false, useMatrixMembers?}`): phases in order `owner` → `tombstones` (repairs only DANGLING stubs — pointer resolves to nothing in the whole vault — and only on a strong key, writing the note id + `prism_merged_into_prev`; a name match → review; a stub merged into a non-person note is left alone; a repaired stub is not repointed in the same run) → `repoint` → `emails` (exact address only; a role mailbox never links whoever holds it (`role-address-claimed`); bulk links counted in `extra.bulkLinked` + `sample.bulk`, `excludeBulkLinks` turns them off; never the owner) → `meetings` (owner linked) → `threads` (by Matrix id; the membership lookup is ON when a matrix credential exists and back-fills `participantIds` in the same write; budget/failure/breaker → the thread WAITS, never a fallback to names; the real member count gates links and ids) → `tasks` (owner linked by configured alias) → `normalize`. Lean per-tag listings + ONE whole-vault lean listing shared by owner/repoint/normalize; every write CAS — no stamp → skipped (`no-stamp`), never `force`; directed "already linked"; removals after additions; ≤2 writes in flight; hard write cap; `PEOPLE_LINK_MAX_CONSECUTIVE_ERRORS` failed writes abort the run as `error`; every vault call times out (`vaultClient(id, {timeoutMs})`); live collab notes get `markReconciled`; `byEvidence` per phase; **`enqueue` defaults FALSE for every run** — with `true` rows are written only inside a capped run's write window and never past `PEOPLE_QUEUE_MAX_OPEN` (1000) open rows, and a landed link closes the rows that asked about it. **Merge** `src/people-merge.ts`: `GET /duplicates` (cached), `POST /merge {personIds, canonicalId, expect:{canonicalUpdatedAt, secondaryUpdatedAt}, confirmUnrelated?, dryRun=true}` — a write needs a human origin, the reviewed versions, a non-weak pair on a FRESH detection (or `confirmUnrelated`), and never takes the owner as secondary; "resume" skips the pair check only when the canonical's `prism_merge_history` (written in step 1 of every merge) names the secondary; `merged_into` is stored as the note id; the secondary is stripped only against a RE-READ of the canonical; metadata edits (`src/people-metadata.ts`) are append-only, send complete nested objects, skip unexpected types, and strip from the secondary only what the canonical verifiably holds; a body is not appended while either note is live (`prism_merged_from` marks it); never deletes; resumable. **Forward linking** (`src/people-forward.ts`, strong keys only, link rides in the note's own write): `PROTON_LINK_RECIPIENTS`, `TRANSCRIPT_LINK_PEOPLE`, `CLICKUP_LINK_ENABLED`, `MATRIX_LINK_EXISTING`, `MATRIX_STORE_PARTICIPANT_IDS`, `PEOPLE_QUEUE_ON_INGEST` (also feeds calendar attendees and Matrix DM counterparts into the queue). **People directory:** `GET /api/people` hides tombstones + non-human notes; a tombstone id opens its canonical with `mergedFrom`. **Health:** `people-link` in `/acl/workers` (queue depth + last job; never stale; failing after 3 errored jobs). Tests: `people-parity`, `identity`, `relationships`, `identity-queue`, `people-link-job`, `people-forward`, `people-merge`, `people-hidden`, `people-health`. **Agent tools (Prism MCP, `mcp/tool-people.ts`, server owner only):** `prism_people_review_queue`/`_review_context`/`_duplicates`/`_link_status` (read) and `prism_people_review_decide`/`_recommend_merge`/`_file_review` (write); hosted turns get them ONLY in the explicit `prism-graph` profile (`AGENT_PRISM_PROFILES` + `AGENT_GRAPH_PROFILE`), never in `prism-ro`/`-rw`/`-suggest`. They call the shared `people-review-service.ts` (the routes call it too), never the admin router; an agent filing never modifies a system row (`exists`); decide is judged against the immutable `system_candidate_ids`/`system_reason` (one row → one system candidate, `add_identity` refused on name-only evidence, merged-away candidate → `conflict`, optional `expect_updated_at` CAS), agent-filed + tombstone rows owner-only, per-credential AND per-account daily caps (`PEOPLE_AGENT_*_PER_DAY`, checked synchronously with the ledger reservation), agent-filed rows capped separately (`PEOPLE_QUEUE_MAX_AGENT_OPEN`), busy → `conflict`; author-written source text comes back inside `untrusted_source`; a recommendation is a SQLite row (`people-agent-store.ts`), never a merge; rationale lives in the `people_agent_decisions` ledger, audit rows stay ids/hashes. Skill drafts: `docs/skills/`. Test: `mcp-people`.

**Calendar ingest (WP1.3) — 🔒 it DELETES notes.** Server port of `calendar_sync.rs` + `calendar_sync_range`: `apps/server/src/worker/calendar.ts`. Same `gog calendar list --from F --to T --max N --account A --json` argv (fake `GogRunner` in tests — **never run real gog from a non-GUI shell**: it reads OAuth from the macOS keychain, which works under pm2 but not a plain ssh/agent shell), same `google` credential as Gmail/Docs. Worker window = UTC today−3d…today+31d, max 250, one pass per `CALENDAR_INTERVAL_MS` slot (5 min); `POST /api/calendar/sync?from=YYYY-MM-DD&to=YYYY-MM-DD` (admin session; replaces the Tauri command; max 100; strict dates, ≤400 days) — both reconcile their window. **Note shape is the desktop's**: `vault/meetings/<start[0..10]|unknown>/<rustSanitizePath(summary)>`, tag `meeting`, metadata `type,title,calendarEventId,date,start,end,attendees,location,meetLink,htmlLink,event_status` (PATCH-merged; body written only on create), attendees → `people.ts` → `attended-by` links, transcript auto-link (`has-transcript`, same score). Found by `calendarEventId`, then path. Reconcile = desktop's: a calendar-synced note in the window whose event id is missing → template-only (nothing under the last "Meeting Notes") **deleted**, otherwise **soft-cancelled** (`event_status: cancelled`); hand-made notes (no `calendarEventId`), already-cancelled and out-of-window notes untouched; **truncation guard** (events ≥ max → skip).
- **Gates.** `CALENDAR_SYNC_ENABLED` (default off). `CALENDAR_SHADOW=true` — fetch + diff, **zero vault writes** (no notes, people, links, cancels, deletes; reads only) and it **wins over ENABLED**. `CALENDAR_DELETE_MODE=log|archive|delete` (default/typo → `log`): `log` records every delete/cancel it would do and writes neither; `archive` applies cancels and turns each delete into tag `calendar-archived` + `event_status: cancelled` + `archivedAt`/`archivedReason` (hidden on the calendar; un-archived automatically if the event reappears); `delete` = the desktop. `CALENDAR_MAX_ORPHANS_PER_PASS` (25; 0 = off) — above it archive/delete apply nothing that pass (intents `blocked`). Reconcile also stands down on a `nextPageToken` or an unrecognised gog shape (the desktop read `{}` as "zero events" → would delete the window).
- **Deliberate deviations (all write/delete less).** Unchanged metadata + links → **no write** (the desktop PATCHed every event every 5 min = a history version each on vault ≥0.7.9). Creates use `if_exists: "ignore"` and only adopt a same-event note. Path fallback never takes a note owned by another event in the same response (two same-titled events on one day flip-flopped under the desktop). Attendee ↔ own email (the desktop zipped misaligned lists). Transcript link also skipped when `transcriptNoteId` is set.
- **Intents.** Every create/update/unarchive/delete/cancel/person-create/link-transcript/skip-collision is persisted (last `CALENDAR_INTENTS_KEEP`=500, settings cursor `cursor:calendar-intents:<vault>`; last pass summary `cursor:calendar-last-pass:<vault>`), each with `effect` = `shadow|logged|applied|archived|blocked|failed`. `GET /acl/workers/calendar/intents?vault=primary&action=delete,cancel&limit=N&verify=1` (server owner) returns them newest-first plus, with `verify=1`, a READ-ONLY recheck of every delete/cancel target: `now` = `gone | cancelled | archived | present`. Log lines: `[calendar] primary …: N events → +c ~u =s · orphans …` and one `reconcile would delete|cancel <id> <path> (<date>)` per orphan.
- **Health.** Live → `calendar` is a SERVER source (stale after `WORKER_STALE_CALENDAR_SERVER_MS`, 1h) and the desktop inference is dropped; shadow → a separate `calendar-shadow` server source, `calendar` stays desktop-inferred.
- **Cutover runbook (overseer only).** (1) **Shadow ≥24h alongside the desktop**: `CALENDAR_SHADOW=true` (+ `google` credential stored, `gog calendar list` working as the pm2 user) → restart pm2. Compare: every few hours pull `GET /acl/workers/calendar/intents?action=delete,cancel&verify=1`. Agreement = every server `delete` intent's `now` becomes `gone` and every `cancel` becomes `cancelled` after the desktop's next 5-min pass; a `present` that stays present means the server would remove something the desktop keeps (**stop, investigate**). The reverse direction — desktop removals the server did not intend — comes from the vault: notes that vanished (`GET /api/notes?tag=meeting&limit=50000 | jq '[.[].id]'` snapshots diffed across the day) or newly `event_status: cancelled` notes, minus the server's intent ids; any id left over is a disagreement. Also: `update`/`create` shadow intents should be ~0 in steady state (the desktop keeps the notes current); a persistent `update` for the same note means a field-port mismatch. (2) **Cutover**: `disable_calendar_sync=true` in `prism-config.json` → restart Prism.app → `CALENDAR_SHADOW=false`, `CALENDAR_SYNC_ENABLED=true`, `CALENDAR_DELETE_MODE=log` → restart pm2 → watch two intervals: `+created` only for genuinely new events; dup check (must print nothing) `…/api/notes?tag=meeting&limit=50000 | jq -r '.[].metadata.calendarEventId // empty' | sort | uniq -d`. (3) After a clean day of `log` intents that match expectations → `CALENDAR_DELETE_MODE=archive`. **Never `delete` without the owner's explicit decision.** Rollback: `CALENDAR_SYNC_ENABLED=false` → restart pm2 → `disable_calendar_sync=false` → restart the desktop; archived notes are recoverable by removing the `calendar-archived` tag.
